| 92 | 600_1 | 92 | 83 | problem | left open | Gegeben $x=\varphi(u,v)$ $y=\psi(u,v)$ $z=\chi(u,v)$, wo $\varphi, \psi, \chi$ ratio- nale Funktionen sind. Man soll für $u, v$, ratio- nale Funktionen von $r, s$, einführen, so dass $r, s$, rationale Funktionen von $x, y, z$, werden. | Given x = φ(u,v), y = ψ(u,v), z = χ(u,v), where φ, ψ, χ are rational functions. One is to introduce rational functions of r, s for u, v, such that r, s become rational functions of x, y, z. | Notes on series of rational functions representing analytic functions, the problem of rational parametrization of surfaces (solved by Castelnuovo in a special case), equilibrium figures of two rotating fluid masses, and Euler's view that impenetrability is the cause of all forces. |
| 93 | 600_1 | 93 | [84] | problem | left open | Suche zu beweisen, dass die Umkehrung einer stetigen, nicht differenzirbaren Funktion stets unendlich vieldeutig ist. | Try to prove that the inverse of a continuous, non-differentiable function is always infinitely many-valued. | The page records a task to prove that the inverse of a continuous nowhere-differentiable function is infinitely many-valued, a plan for a non-Gaussian probability theory modelled on non-Euclidean geometry, and a sketch showing that when the resultant of n forms is nonzero the variables are integral algebraic functions of the forms. |
| 94 | 600_1 | 94 | 85 | problem | left open | Man soll die Criterien, damit die Form $F \equiv 0$ $(M)$ sei in eine [-Form-] Gestalt bringen so dass sie von der Ordnung der Form $F$ unabhängig sei. | One should bring the criteria for the form F ≡ 0 (M) into a shape such that they are independent of the order of the form F. | A dismissive remark on Christoffel's essay on irrational numbers, followed by a research programme on Noether's general theorem about surfaces through given points and curves, membership of a form in a module system, and a link to ideal theory. |
| 95 | 600_1 | 114 | 105 | problem | left open | Endlich entsteht noch das Problem ob zu jedem gegebenen algebraischen Verzweigungsgebilde bei vorgeschriebnem Ver- zweigungsschema auch wirklich ein zugehöri- ges algebr. Gebilde existirt. | Finally there also arises the problem whether, for every given algebraic branching configuration with a prescribed branching scheme, an associated algebraic configuration actually exists. | Defines a first and second genus (p_1, p_2) of a space by the number of cut surfaces needed so that every closed surface, respectively every closed curve, can be contracted to a point, raises the existence problem for algebraic configurations with a prescribed branching scheme (a generalisation of Riemann's existence theorem), and lists coloured relations between three functions U, V, W. |
| 96 | 600_1 | 116 | 107 | problem | left open | Man kann von der Invariantentheorie der binären Formen ganz loslösen das Problem der Aufstellung aller ganzen rationalen Funk- tionen, welche bei Anwendung einer einzi- gen linearen ganzzahligen Substitution d.h. Aufstellung de[-sjenigen-] vollen Systems solcher Invarianten. | One can completely detach from the invariant theory of binary forms the problem of determining all integral rational functions which [remain unchanged] under application of a single linear integral substitution, i.e. determining the full system of such invariants. | Hilbert proposes reducing a set of conditions by considering moduli of the form (φ, ψ, (x−a)^λ(y−b)^μ), then remarks that finding all integral rational functions invariant under a single integral linear substitution can be separated from binary invariant theory, and that invariance under infinitely many substitutions essentially yields only semi-invariants. |
| 97 | 600_1 | 119 | [110] | problem | left open | Als erläuterndes Beispiel diene das Problem: Gesucht wird ein binäres Formenbüschel, dessen Funktionaldeterminante gegeben ist. | As an illustrative example, let the following problem serve: a binary pencil of forms is sought whose functional determinant is given. | Applying the concept of a full system of forms to algebraic problems (systems of polynomial equations), illustrated by the problem of finding a binary pencil of forms with a given functional determinant, followed by the start of a question about full invariant systems. |
| 98 | 600_1 | 120 | 111 | problem | left open | Geg. eine ternäre Form 4ter Ordnung gesucht 3 ternäre quadra= tische Formen, so dass $f = \varphi\psi - \chi^2$ (Problem der Doppeltangenten einer Curve 4ter Ordnung.) | Given a ternary form of 4th order, find 3 ternary quadratic forms such that f = φψ − χ² (problem of the bitangents of a curve of 4th order). | Notes on superfluous invariants of binary forms, on computing the characteristic function for ternary forms (in particular expressing a ternary quartic as $\varphi\psi-\chi^2$, the bitangent problem), and on extending the Cayley-Sylvester counting theorem to ternary forms. |
| 99 | 600_1 | 138 | 129 | problem | left open | Darstellung einer ganzen positiven Zahl als Summe von Quadrat[-en-] und Cubus. | Representation of a positive integer as the sum of a square and a cube. | A list of research problems: proving Dirichlet's habilitation theorems with ideal theory, representing integers as a square plus a cube via discriminants of cubic fields and complex multiplication, solving trinomial equations with modular functions, the class number of pure cubic fields, and a question about composing quadratic fields. |
| 100 | 600_1 | 138 | 129 | problem | left open | Die Auflösung der trinomischen Gleichung $x^m + x^n t + t = 0$ durch Modulfunctionen. | The solution of the trinomial equation $x^m + x^n t + t = 0$ by modular functions. | A list of research problems: proving Dirichlet's habilitation theorems with ideal theory, representing integers as a square plus a cube via discriminants of cubic fields and complex multiplication, solving trinomial equations with modular functions, the class number of pure cubic fields, and a question about composing quadratic fields. |
| 101 | 600_1 | 139 | [130] | problem | left open | Alle Gleichungen 4ten Grades mit rationalen Coefficienten und der Discriminante $\pm 1$ zu ermitteln. Zurückführen auf Gl. 3ten Grades, indem man die Darstell. | To determine all equations of the 4th degree with rational coefficients and discriminant $\pm 1$. Reduce to equations of the 3rd degree by [taking] the represent[ation]... | Research notes listing three tasks: determining class numbers of multiquadratic fields (especially biquadratic fields), defining the lemniscate function by algebraic approximation following a hint by Gauss, and finding all quartic equations with rational coefficients and discriminant ±1. |
| 102 | 600_1 | 140 | 131 | problem | left open | 1) Existenz der Klassenkörper vom Relativgrade $2^{n}$ in Bezug auf $k(\sqrt{pq})$, indem man die Einheiten in diesem Körper und in den Körper $k(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{q})$ studirt. | 1) Existence of class fields of relative degree 2^n with respect to k(√pq), by studying the units in this field and in the field k(√p, √q). | A list of research and dissertation topics: the discriminant and invariants of the quartic, Poincaré cycles of differential equations, rotation of a body filled with fluid, three number-theoretic doctoral topics (class fields, Fermat's theorem, Weber's theorem), and independence of the laws 1-17 for complex number systems. |
| 103 | 600_1 | 140 | 131 | problem | left open | 2.) Beweis des Fermat'schen Satzes für den Fall dass die Klassenanzahl des Körpers $k(\sqrt[l]{1})$ durch $l$ aber nicht durch $l^2$ (vielleicht auch durch $l^2$) teilbar ist, nach meiner einfacheren Methode unter Annahme der Reziprozitätsgesetze für $l^{te}$ Potenzreste | 2.) Proof of Fermat's theorem for the case that the class number of the field k(l-th root of 1) is divisible by l but not by l^2 (perhaps also by l^2), by my simpler method, assuming the reciprocity laws for l-th power residues. | A list of research and dissertation topics: the discriminant and invariants of the quartic, Poincaré cycles of differential equations, rotation of a body filled with fluid, three number-theoretic doctoral topics (class fields, Fermat's theorem, Weber's theorem), and independence of the laws 1-17 for complex number systems. |
| 104 | 600_1 | 140 | 131 | problem | left open | 3.) Beweis des Weber'schen Satzes, dass die Idealklassenanzahl[?] des Körpers $\sqrt[2^h]{1}$ ungerade ist | 3.) Proof of Weber's theorem that the (ideal) class number of the field of the 2^h-th root of 1 is odd. | A list of research and dissertation topics: the discriminant and invariants of the quartic, Poincaré cycles of differential equations, rotation of a body filled with fluid, three number-theoretic doctoral topics (class fields, Fermat's theorem, Weber's theorem), and independence of the laws 1-17 for complex number systems. |
| 105 | 600_1 | 142 | 133 | problem | left open | Die Eulersche diophantische Aufgabe. Siehe Legendre Zahlentheorie S. 140 Band 2. | Euler's Diophantine problem. See Legendre, Number Theory, p. 140, vol. 2. | A list of research problems and plans: shortening Kummer's proof in cyclotomic fields, extending the Pascal hexagon's notable points, Euler's Diophantine problem, division of the lemniscate arc into a+ib parts, transferring Staude's string construction of the ellipsoid, and treating the fields √(a+bi) and ∛α to derive reciprocity laws. |
| 106 | 600_1 | 142 | 133 | problem | left open | Geometrische Bedeutung der Theilung des Lemnis- catenbogens in $a+ib$ gleiche Theile und geome- trische Construction derselben, falls dies möglich ist, vergl. Jacobi Bd 6 S 276. | Geometric meaning of dividing the lemniscate arc into a+ib equal parts, and the geometric construction of it, if this is possible; compare Jacobi vol. 6 p. 276. | A list of research problems and plans: shortening Kummer's proof in cyclotomic fields, extending the Pascal hexagon's notable points, Euler's Diophantine problem, division of the lemniscate arc into a+ib parts, transferring Staude's string construction of the ellipsoid, and treating the fields √(a+bi) and ∛α to derive reciprocity laws. |
| 107 | 600_2 | 4 | 0 | problem | left open | Stelle auf eine Theorie des Rechnens d. h. ergründe worin die Vortheile bekann= | Set up a theory of calculation, i.e. find out wherein the advantages of the know[n] ... (continues on next page). | Three short notes: proving that the Bernoulli function (polynomial) is irreducible, using its behaviour modulo a prime degree p; a remark that an ideal probability device (wheel of fortune) is impossible; and a task to set up a theory of calculation. |
| 108 | 600_2 | 6 | 1 | problem | left open | zu dem Problem zu beweisen, dass jede Function welche das Innere eines Ovals einer algebrai= schen Curve auf eine Halbebene abbildet einer gewöhnlichen algebraischen Differentialgleichung genügt. | to the problem of proving that every function which maps the interior of an oval of an algebraic curve onto a half-plane satisfies an ordinary algebraic differential equation. | Prompted by a lecture of Lindemann, Hilbert considers whether every function mapping the interior of an oval of an algebraic curve onto a half-plane satisfies an ordinary algebraic differential equation, and describes Lindemann's approach and its limitation. |
| 109 | 600_2 | 12 | 7 | problem | left open | Entwickele durch einfaches Ausrechnen $e^{\pi} = i^{i}$ in 7. einen gewöhnlichen Kettenbruch und siehe, ob ein Gesetz kommt. | Expand $e^{\pi} = i^{i}$ by simple calculation into an ordinary continued fraction and see whether a law emerges. | Notes posing several problems: expanding e^π = i^i into a simple continued fraction, a reduction of a problem of Lindemann's to finding rational functions invariant under a substitution, the meaning of Jacobi's inversion problem and Abel's theorem for genus p = ∞, and the behaviour of the second root when a root is approximated by a quadratic equation. |
| 110 | 600_2 | 12 | 7 | problem | left open | Gegeben ist eine rationale Funktion R von U und z. Man soll eine rationale Funk- tion mit reellen Coefficienten von $U, U' = \frac{dU}{dz}, U'', \ldots$ und z bestimmen, welche in sich übergeht, wenn man $U = R(U, z)$ setzt. | Given a rational function R of U and z. One is to determine a rational function with real coefficients of U, U' = dU/dz, U'', ... and z that is transformed into itself when one sets U = R(U, z). | Notes posing several problems: expanding e^π = i^i into a simple continued fraction, a reduction of a problem of Lindemann's to finding rational functions invariant under a substitution, the meaning of Jacobi's inversion problem and Abel's theorem for genus p = ∞, and the behaviour of the second root when a root is approximated by a quadratic equation. |
| 111 | 600_2 | 16 | 11 | problem | left open | Suche die Integralformel für das Potential eines Ellipsoids (vergl. Schlömilch Compendium Bd 2. S. 516. Aus den Eigenschaften der ana- lytischen Functionen abzuleiten, indem du zeigt dass das Integral für den aeusseren Punkt eine lineare Function von $\alpha$, für den inne- ren Punkt gleich $\alpha$ einem elliptischen In- tegral werden muss. | Find the integral formula for the potential of an ellipsoid (cf. Schlömilch, Compendium, vol. 2, p. 516). Derive it from the properties of analytic functions by showing that the integral must become a linear function of alpha for the exterior point, and equal to alpha times an elliptic integral for the interior point. | The page covers the maximum multiplicity of inflection points of a plane curve of order n, with the example x^n+y^n+1=0. It then sets a task to derive the integral formula for the potential of an ellipsoid from properties of analytic functions, and begins a question about conditions for the maximum or minimum of an algebraic function. |
| 112 | 600_2 | 20 | 15 | problem | left open | Bestimme die Anzahl der Primzahlen unter- halb einer Grenze, für welche etwa die Zahl 2 eine primitive Wurzel ist. | Determine the number of primes below a bound for which, say, the number 2 is a primitive root. | Research plans: studying prime ideals following Kronecker via the field y^2+1=0, re-proving Gauss's representation of primes as sums of two squares via the function field x^2+y^2=p, proving Dirichlet's unit theorem without the existence theorem for roots, and counting primes for which 2 is a primitive root. |
| 113 | 600_2 | 20 | 15 | problem | left open | Suche den Dirichletschen Satz von den Ein- heiten eines Körpers ohne Benutzung des Exis- tenzsatzes der Wurzeln nachzuweisen. | Try to prove Dirichlet's theorem on the units of a field without using the existence theorem for roots. | Research plans: studying prime ideals following Kronecker via the field y^2+1=0, re-proving Gauss's representation of primes as sums of two squares via the function field x^2+y^2=p, proving Dirichlet's unit theorem without the existence theorem for roots, and counting primes for which 2 is a primitive root. |
| 114 | 600_2 | 20 | 15 | problem | left open | Weise vor Allem nach die Unendlichkeit solcher Prim- | Above all, prove the infinitude of such primes. | Research plans: studying prime ideals following Kronecker via the field y^2+1=0, re-proving Gauss's representation of primes as sums of two squares via the function field x^2+y^2=p, proving Dirichlet's unit theorem without the existence theorem for roots, and counting primes for which 2 is a primitive root. |
| 115 | 600_2 | 27 | [22] | problem | left open | Gauss'sche Problem: Bestimmung der Verhältnisse der Häufig- keit der irregulären negativen Determinanten zur Häufig- keit der regulären. | Gauss's problem: determination of the ratio of the frequency of irregular negative determinants to the frequency of regular ones. | Short notes on Gauss's problem about how often irregular and regular negative determinants occur, a claim that a continuous series-sum need not converge uniformly, a preference for Weierstrass's convergence method over Eisenstein–Kronecker's, and the start of a discussion of irrational numbers defined by fundamental sequences. |
| 116 | 600_2 | 37 | [32] | problem | left open | Zeige, dass man durch aufeinanderfolgende beliebige An- wendung der Axiome niemals zu widersprechenden Sätzen gelangen kann. | Show that by successive arbitrary application of the axioms one can never arrive at contradictory theorems. | Hilbert notes that all intersection-point theorems should follow from closure theorems, plans to list such theorems for circles, conics and quadrics, and sketches how to prove the consistency of the axioms by analytic means. |
| 117 | 600_2 | 48 | 43 | problem | left open | Das Wichtig= ste ist zunächst zu zeigen, dass die Differential= gleichung keine anderen kritischen Punkte als $z = 0, 1, \infty$ hat. | The most important thing is first to show that the differential equation has no critical points other than $z = 0, 1, \infty$. | The page identifies certain recursively defined expressions with the Sturm functions (the last being the discriminant) and then sets up the continued-fraction expansion of $(1-z)^\alpha$ via a second-order linear differential equation for an auxiliary function. |
| 118 | 600_2 | 52 | 47 | problem | left open | Beweise dass es keine andere Zahl im Kreis- körper giebt ausser der Einheitswurzel selbst deren Diskriminante gleich der Diskriminan- te des Körpers ist. | Prove that there is no number in the cyclotomic field other than the root of unity itself whose discriminant equals the discriminant of the field. | Three short notes: Minkowski's conjecture that a closed surface of flexible but inextensible material cannot be continuously bent (especially the sphere); whether all ruler-and-compass constructions can be done with compass alone; and a task to prove that no number in the cyclotomic field other than the root of unity has discriminant equal to the field discriminant. |
| 119 | 600_2 | 53 | [48] | problem | left open | Beweise dass die Gleichung $x^n + \binom{n}{1}^\alpha x^{n-1} + \binom{n}{2}^\alpha x^{n-2} + \dots + 1 = 0$ für $\alpha > 1$ lauter reelle, für $\alpha < 1$ lauter [-kleine-] imaginäre Wurzeln hat. ausser einer für ungerades $n$. | Prove that the equation x^n + (n choose 1)^alpha x^{n-1} + (n choose 2)^alpha x^{n-2} + ... + 1 = 0 has only real roots for alpha > 1, and only imaginary roots for alpha < 1, except for one when n is odd. | Two exercises: proving that a polynomial with binomial coefficients raised to a power alpha has all real roots for alpha > 1 and all imaginary roots for alpha < 1, and a plan to prove the irrationality/transcendence of e and pi without integrals by replacing the integral with the limit of a sum. |
| 120 | 600_2 | 54 | 49 | problem | left open | ebenso zeige, dass man von der Curve $x^4+y^4=1$ unend lich viele Punkte mit linearen Zirkeln, d.h. durch Wurzelziehn construiren kann und ebenso $\infty$ viele Punkte der Fläche 5ter Ord. $x^5+y^5+z^5=1$. (mit Hilfe der [-Linien[?]-] Haupttangenten). | likewise show that one can construct infinitely many points of the curve x^4+y^4=1 with linear circles, i.e. by extracting roots, and likewise infinitely many points of the 5th-order surface x^5+y^5+z^5=1 (with the help of the principal tangents). | A remark that an extensive theory is not a fault even if it is needed to prove a simple theorem, with an analogy to a letter and its reply, followed by notes on finding infinitely many rational points on cubic curves by tangent construction and on extending this to x^4+y^4=1, the quintic surface x^5+y^5+z^5=1, and the diophantine problems of Euler and Legendre. |
| 121 | 600_2 | 75 | [70] | problem | left open | In weiter Ferne taucht dann einmal das Problem auf alle nicht auflösbaren Gleichungen 5ten Grades mit vorgeschriebener Diskriminante zu construiren. | In the far distance there then appears the problem of constructing all non-solvable equations of the 5th degree with prescribed discriminant. | Short notes on constructing all non-solvable quintic equations with prescribed discriminant, on when continuation of potentials is permitted, and remarks on the critical period in mathematics and on general theories. |
| 122 | 600_2 | 76 | 71 | problem | left open | Beweise dass das System aller rationalen Zahlen zwischen 0 und 1, so dass auch noch $\frac{1}{2}$ sich selbst entspricht, umkehrbar eindeutig und [-Aeq-] stetig {+und so dass ein Differentialquotient existirt+} (d.h. so dass zwei immer alle innerhalb [?] wieder 2 grössere entsprechen) nur so auf einander bezogen werden können, dass jede rationale Zahl sich selbst entspricht | Prove that the system of all rational numbers between 0 and 1 can be mapped onto itself one-to-one and continuously (and so that a differential quotient exists), with 1/2 also corresponding to itself (i.e. so that to two [numbers] always two larger ones correspond again), only in such a way that every rational number corresponds to itself | Notes on the theory of fields as a theory of the four arithmetic operations (Körper, Feld, Ring, Gitter), a claim that the only one-to-one, continuous, differentiable map of the rationals between 0 and 1 onto themselves is a linear fractional function, and the finiteness of the number of ideal classes for a field of algebraic functions of two variables. |
| 123 | 600_2 | 76 | 71 | problem | left open | Beweise zu dem Zweck, dass wenn auf einer Fläche $f=0$ eine Curve $N^{te}$ Ordnung gegeben ist, stets eine [-C-] Curve von einer kleineren Ordnung als $N$ gefunden werden kann, so dass diese mit der früheren Curve den vollen [-Sy-] Schnitt von $f=0$ mit einer anderen Fläche bildet —— vorausgesetzt, dass $N > \#$ ist als eine nur von der Fläche $f=0$ abhängigen Grenze | For this purpose prove that if a curve of order N is given on a surface f=0, a curve of order smaller than N can always be found such that together with the earlier curve it forms the full intersection of f=0 with another surface — provided that N exceeds a bound depending only on the surface f=0 | Notes on the theory of fields as a theory of the four arithmetic operations (Körper, Feld, Ring, Gitter), a claim that the only one-to-one, continuous, differentiable map of the rationals between 0 and 1 onto themselves is a linear fractional function, and the finiteness of the number of ideal classes for a field of algebraic functions of two variables. |
| 124 | 600_2 | 77 | [72] | problem | left open | Beweise, dass durch die Theilung der Abel'schen Functionen alle Körper ge- funden werden können, welche in Bezug auf einen algebraischen Functionenkörper relativ Abel'sch sind. | Prove that, by the division of Abelian functions, all fields can be found which are relatively Abelian with respect to an algebraic function field. | Three research notes: simultaneous rational approximation of (1-z)^α and (1-z)^{-α} as a route to proving 2^√2 irrational; obtaining all relatively Abelian fields over an algebraic function field by division of Abelian functions; and a claim, with the start of a proof, that every small region of a Riemann surface contains points whose product as prime ideals lies in a given class. |
| 125 | 600_2 | 79 | [74] | problem | left open | Beweise, dass die Gl. 6ten Grades durch Wurzelzeichen niemals auf einen Parameter gebracht werden kann. | Prove that the equation of degree 6 can never be brought by means of radicals to one parameter. | The page sketches how to show, without Riemann's integral, that the ξ-function has genus 1 as a function of t², asks whether the prime factors of Frobenius's group determinant are themselves determinants, and poses a problem on reducing the sextic equation by radicals to one or two parameters. |
| 126 | 600_2 | 86 | 81 | problem | left open | Es ist ein Problem, wie man durch möglichst wenige Constructionen $\pi$ so möglichst genau construiren kann? | It is a problem how one can construct π as accurately as possible with as few constructions as possible. | Hilbert lists groups of geometric axioms (incidence, congruence/motion, continuity/Archimedean, Desargues, special parallel axiom), states equivalences among combinations of them in the plane and in space, and then poses the problem of constructing π (and the cube root of 2) as accurately as possible with as few construction operations as possible. |
| 127 | 600_2 | 89 | [84] | problem | left open | Beweise den Satz: Eine quinäre quadratische Diophantische Gl. ist in dem durch ihre Coefficienten be- stimmten Rationalitätsbe- reiche stets lösbar, wenn sie in dem bez. reellen Körper nicht definit ist. | Prove the theorem: a quinary quadratic Diophantine equation is always solvable in the domain of rationality determined by its coefficients if it is not definite in the corresponding real field. | A pasted-in plan for Hilbert's future number-theoretic studies (relative abelian fields, Fermat's theorem, complex multiplication), followed by notes on solvability of quadratic and higher-degree Diophantine equations, a remark on Molien's paper on hypercomplex numbers, and an elementary proof of the arithmetic–geometric mean inequality after Hurwitz. |
| 128 | 600_2 | 89 | [84] | problem | left open | Kriterien für die Transfor- mirbarkeit quadratischer Formen. | Criteria for the transformability of quadratic forms. | A pasted-in plan for Hilbert's future number-theoretic studies (relative abelian fields, Fermat's theorem, complex multiplication), followed by notes on solvability of quadratic and higher-degree Diophantine equations, a remark on Molien's paper on hypercomplex numbers, and an elementary proof of the arithmetic–geometric mean inequality after Hurwitz. |
| 129 | 600_2 | 91 | [86] | problem | left open | fällt Entfernungen $\geqq 1$ haben. Schon in der Ebene schwierig. | ...drops [perpendiculars] have distances ≥ 1. Already difficult in the plane. | The page ends a note on a geometric distance problem, records claims that x^2+2=y^n and x^2+5=y^n have no integer solutions, and states Minkowski's theorem on convex surfaces with prescribed Gaussian curvature as a function of the normal, with a sketch of the plane case. |
| 130 | 600_2 | 96 | 91 | problem | left open | Aber schon das Problem, ob zwischen abzählbar und Linearconti- nuum etwas zwischen liegt sehr schwer. | But already the problem whether something lies between the countable and the linear continuum is very hard. | Reflecting on Cantor's letters, Hilbert considers the problem of which collections may be regarded as 'finished' (complete) in set theory, and proposes formulating set-theoretic propositions without the word 'set' and treating terms such as 'irrational number' merely as names for things with specified properties. |
| 131 | 600_2 | 112 | 107 | problem | left open | Das Hermannsche Problem ist die Entzifferung von Melodien, Partituren, aller Instrumente, des Textes, allein aus der resultirenden phono- graphischen Curve. | Hermann's problem is the deciphering of melodies, scores, all instruments, and the text, solely from the resulting phonographic curve. | General philosophical remarks on mathematics: how new sciences emerge, the role of mathematics in physics and the proposal to axiomatize mechanics and optics on the model of non-Euclidean geometry, 'Hermann's problem' of reconstructing music from a phonograph curve, and a 'hymn to the circle'. |
| 132 | 600_3 | 20 | 17 | problem | left open | Beweise, dass z. B. die Differentialgl. $y''-xy=0$ {+oder $y'+xy^2=0$?+} (sei[?] nicht durch Quadraturen lösbar ist | Prove that, e.g., the differential equation y'' - xy = 0 (or y' + xy^2 = 0?) is not solvable by quadratures. | The page gives an identity writing every fourth power as a cube plus two squares, lists Poincaré's papers on Analysis situs, and sketches a plan to prove that certain differential equations such as y'' - xy = 0 cannot be solved by quadratures. |
| 133 | 600_3 | 21 | [18] | problem | left open | es schon eine sehr schwierige Aufgabe ist, bloss zu beweisen, dass unsere Differentialg. [-diese-] durch [-e-] bloss eine Quadratur nicht lösbar ist | it is already a very difficult task merely to prove that our differential equation is not solvable by just one quadrature | The page discusses how hard it is to prove that a differential equation cannot be solved by a single quadrature, then asks how solids, liquids, gases, aether and electricity should be described under molecular theory using mean values and expansions in small parameters, and finally states that the function zeta(s,x) = sum x^n/n^s satisfies no known differential equation. |
| 134 | 600_3 | 22 | 19 | problem | left open | Beweise die Unmöglichkeit der Verbiegung einer beliebigen (positiv- oder positiv-negativgekrümmten Fläche vermöge der Unmöglichkeit einer Funktion $\vartheta$, die der Gl. $\frac{\partial^2\vartheta}{\partial u\partial v} =$ $M\vartheta$ bez. $\frac{\partial^2\vartheta}{\partial u^2}+\frac{\partial^2\vartheta}{\partial v^2} = M\vartheta$ genügt | Prove the impossibility of bending an arbitrary (positively or positively-negatively curved) surface by means of the impossibility of a function ϑ satisfying the equation ∂²ϑ/∂u∂v = Mϑ, resp. ∂²ϑ/∂u² + ∂²ϑ/∂v² = Mϑ. | Notes on proving that the function ζ(s,x) satisfies no algebraic partial differential equation, a suggested proof of the non-bendability of surfaces via the equation ∂²ϑ/∂u∂v = Mϑ (citing Bianchi), and a pasted note on Cantor's normal form of ordinals with an attempt to prove the continuum has the second cardinality. |
| 135 | 600_3 | 24 | 21 | problem | left open | Was den functionentheoretischen Theil des Problems betrifft: Ausdehnung{+ + +} des Kroneckerschen Theorems, dass alle Abelschen Körper Kreiskörper sind | As regards the function-theoretic part of the problem: extension of Kronecker's theorem that all Abelian fields are cyclotomic fields | The page ends a note citing Lie's claim that every real transformation can be regarded as generated by an infinitesimal one, then proposes a method for the function-theoretic part of extending Kronecker's theorem that all Abelian fields are cyclotomic fields: first determine number fields of relative degree a power of 2 whose relative discriminant contains only prime factors dividing 2. |
| 136 | 600_3 | 25 | 22 | problem | left open | Problem: Ein beliebiges Continuum auf das Innere des Kreises abzu- bilden | Problem: to map an arbitrary continuum onto the interior of the circle. | The page completes a remark on determining number fields with Hilbert's own methods, states the problem of mapping an arbitrary continuum onto the interior of a circle and gives a geometric reformulation, and reflects on the importance of number theory and geometric intuition for mathematicians. |

| 141 | 600_3 | 30 | 27 | problem | left open | Aufgabe: Einen Garten in 3 [-gleiche-] inhaltsgleiche Teile so zuteilen, dass die Gesammtlänge der verwandten Zäune ein Minimum wird. | Problem: to divide a garden into 3 parts of equal area in such a way that the total length of the fences used becomes a minimum. | A garden-division minimum problem, quotations from Kronecker's lectures on definite integrals (Darwinian analogy, functions of one variable as limit cases, Fourier double integral, passage to Poisson's integral via Abel's theorem, a convergence theorem for Fourier series), and the Codazzi formulas applied to Hilbert's theorem on surfaces of constant negative curvature. |

| 150 | 600_3 | 88 | 85 | problem | left open | (Aufstellung eines geeigneten allgemeinen Kriteriums dafür, dass ein Syst. ein wirklich bestimmtes ist!) | (Establishing a suitable general criterion for a system to be a genuinely determined one!) | Hilbert discusses when a system of partial differential equations (including a Jacobian-type determinant condition) is genuinely determined, proposes finding a general criterion for this, and then sketches a plan to prove his independence theorem in the calculus of variations by deriving a partial differential equation for the minimum as a function of the endpoint. |

| 176 | 600_1 | 26 | 17 | question | left open | Wie kann man die andern Fälle abbilden? | How can one map the other cases? | The page argues that a definite binary form can be deformed into any other definite form without passing through forms with real zeros, proposes extending Noether's fundamental theorem to systems of forms, and restates Hilbert's theorem that not every definite form is a sum of squares, phrased as a mapping problem. |

| 296 | 600_3 | 53 | [50] | question | left open | Man kann so [-a-] verallgemeinern, dass man beispiels- weise nach {+einer+} ganzen Function $P_n(x)$ fragt so dass $\frac{d^3}{dx^3}\left((x^2-1)P_n^3\right)$ durch $P_n^2$ teilbar wird etc. | One can generalise in such a way that one asks, for example, for a polynomial $P_n(x)$ such that $\frac{d^3}{dx^3}((x^2-1)P_n^3)$ becomes divisible by $P_n^2$, etc. | Continuing earlier developments, Hilbert considers a generalisation involving polynomials $P_n$ and then studies rational (Padé-type) approximation of $e^x$ by $G/H$, deriving a differential identity satisfied by the denominator polynomial $H$. |

| 304 | 600_3 | 63 | [60] | question | left open | Kann man dies Kriterium auch in der Form [-...-] $F+T$ = Min aussprechen, wo $T$ die kinetische Energie ist? | Can one also express this criterion in the form F+T = Min, where T is the kinetic energy? | The page discusses minimum principles and stability criteria in statics and dynamics (F+V, F+T, d'Alembert and Hamilton principles), gives an example differential equation of higher degree with its general integral, and notes a plan to connect Cauchy's method for linear PDEs with constant coefficients to his integral equations and apply it to Maxwell's equations. |
| 305 | 600_3 | 66 | 63 | question | left open | Welches ist [-x-] z.B. im Raume die Greensche Funktion für einen Kreis? | What, for example, is the Green's function for a circle in space? | A note on carrying over function theory systematically to potential theory in space (closed curves, logarithmic singularities, Green's function and boundary value problem for a circle), followed by a pasted slip on proving statements A(wx) for all x by induction-like axioms, the consistency of the system, and the existence of a successor for finite numbers. |

| 307 | 600_3 | 67 | [64] | question | left open | Bewegt sich etwa das Elektron nach einem Minimumsatz und wie ist der Zusammenhang mit dem Helmholtz-Schwarzschild'schen Minimumsprincip? | Does the electron perhaps move according to a minimum theorem, and what is the connection with the Helmholtz–Schwarzschild minimum principle? | The page notes Nernst's diffusion problem with a closed-form solution, a question about two Newtonian mass points colliding along a line, questions about minimum principles in optics and Maxwell's electrodynamics, and the start of a remark on Poincaré's idea of periodic cycles on Riemann surfaces. |

| 309 | 600_3 | 74 | 71 | question | left open | Wie schliessen sich die Elemente an einander? | How do the elements connect to one another? | Hilbert discusses solving Pfaffian-type differential equations without integrals (the 'diophantine' problem) by expressing the unknowns as functions of arbitrary functions and their derivatives, compares this to genus-0 algebraic relations, and considers transcendental relations between pairs of functions defined by partial differential or integral equations. |

| 311 | 600_3 | 77 | [74] | question | left open | Wie heissen die Grundlösungen von $\frac{d^3u}{dx^3}+\frac{\partial u}{\partial y}=0$ ?, $\frac{d^3u}{dx^3}+\frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial y^2}+\frac{\partial u}{\partial z}=0$ nach Analogie der Wärmeleitungsgl. $\frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}+\frac{\partial u}{\partial y}=0$, wo man nach Funktionen $u$, die nur von $\frac{y^2}{x}$ abhängen! | What are the fundamental solutions of $u_{xxx}+u_y=0$?, $u_{xxx}+u_{yy}+u_z=0$, by analogy with the heat equation $u_{xx}+u_y=0$, where one [looks] for functions $u$ that depend only on $y^2/x$! | Hilbert notes that the zeta function can be written as an integral of a product of two functions each satisfying a linear second-order differential equation, asks for the fundamental solutions of certain third-order heat-type equations, and records leftover remarks on integral equations concerning spectral representations of quadratic forms in infinitely many variables. |

| 313 | 600_3 | 90 | 87 | question | left open | Probleme, wie die Frage, ob es eine Fläche $4^{\text{ter}}$ Ordnung mit 11 getrennten Ovalen giebt, oder ob welches die Maximalzahl der sich einander umschliessenden Züge einer ebenen | Problems such as the question whether there exists a surface of 4th order with 11 separate ovals, or what the maximum number of mutually enclosing branches of a plane [curve is] (sentence continues on the next page). | The page finishes an argument that every form near a form expressible as a sum of 2n-th powers of linear forms with positive coefficients is itself so expressible, then sets out a plan to prove the prime number theorem by systematically solving Chebyshev's functional equation, and begins a remark on problems such as quartic surfaces with 11 ovals. |
| 314 | 600_3 | 96 | 93 | question | left open | Die Frage ist also nur die, wo steckt [-der Re-] dieser Rechenfehler? | The question is thus only: where is this calculation error hidden? | Philosophical notes on the Kantian antinomies as calculation errors in the logic calculus, three properties of thought, consistency of axioms within their own domain, axiomatizability as the criterion of science, and a plan to prove Zermelo's axiom of choice consistent. |
| 315 | 600_3 | 98 | 95 | question | left open | Kants fundamentale Fragestellung: Wie ist reine [-r-] Mathematik möglich? | Kant's fundamental question: How is pure mathematics possible? | Philosophical notes on the relation of thought and reality, a proposed theorem that existence proofs imply constructibility, a question about perpetual motion of the second kind in living beings, the role of mathematics and the axiomatic method, and the beginning of a remark on experiments and geometry. |

| 317 | 600_3 | 100 | 97 | question | left open | Denken die Mi[?]- kroben? | Do microbes think? | Hilbert argues that all sciences that produce lasting results ultimately supply material for mathematics, citing Einstein's 1912 letter to Sommerfeld, and proposes grounding logic in physical-mechanical considerations as an invariant kind of 'happening'; he then begins a thesis on applying the laws of probability to logic and mathematical statements. |
| 318 | 600_3 | 102 | 99 | question | left open | sich nur eine Phantasie. weshalb? | only a fantasy. Why? | Philosophical and foundational remarks: a chess analogy, Germany's lead in the theory of number from Dedekind and Cantor's dispute with Kronecker, a relative-consistency formulation of Zermelo's axiom of choice, and the relativity of 'working hypothesis' versus 'real existence' with reference to Planck. |
| 319 | 600_3 | 103 | [100] | question | left open | Ist da zur Kontrolle ein Uebergeist nöthig? | Is a 'super-mind' necessary there for control? | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: its relation to philosophy, logic and the natural sciences; mathematics as experimenting with thoughts; the axiom of choice used as a formal experiment that cannot lead to contradiction; and a proposed task of showing that ordinary theorems found by transfinite principles can also be proved directly. |
| 320 | 600_3 | 103 | [100] | question | left open | wie kommt es, dass nicht jedes Problem gleich [-...-] gelöst | How does it come about that not every problem is immediately solved | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: its relation to philosophy, logic and the natural sciences; mathematics as experimenting with thoughts; the axiom of choice used as a formal experiment that cannot lead to contradiction; and a proposed task of showing that ordinary theorems found by transfinite principles can also be proved directly. |
| 321 | 600_3 | 106 | 103 | question | left open | Was braucht die Physik zu ihrer befriedigenden logischen Be- gründung? | What does physics need for its satisfactory logical foundation? | Hilbert reflects on modern mathematics, which seeks inner understanding through ideas and culminates in the axiomatic method, and then asks what physics needs from mathematics for its progress and for its logical foundation. |
| 322 | 600_3 | 109 | [106] | question | left open | Auf wie viel Procent richtig sind nach dem heutigen Standpunkt der Wissenschaften (Geodaesie, [-A-] Astrono- mie, Physik) die Axiome der Geometrie? | To what percentage are the axioms of geometry correct, from the current standpoint of the sciences (geodesy, astronomy, physics)? | Brief philosophical notes on how accurately the axioms of geometry hold empirically, on theories needing a point, on mathematical existence as freedom from contradiction (with examples of algebraic roots and surfaces of negative curvature), and on Lilienthal's flights refuting Helmholtz's claim about the maximum mass of flying birds. |
| 323 | 600_3 | 117 | [114] | question | left open | Wenn man irgend ein System von Axiomen hat, so kann man fragen, ob [-das-] {+mein+} Vollständigkeitsaxiom überhaupt hinzuzufügen möglich ist d.h. ob es nicht widerspre- chend [?] [-ist-] sei bez. welche Axiome noch vorher hinzugefügt werden müssen, damit [-d-] | If one has any system of axioms, one can ask whether it is possible at all to add my completeness axiom, i.e. whether it is not contradictory, or which axioms must first be added so that [sentence continues on next page]. | Reflections on the axiomatic method: that it unifies branches internally, that old theories reappear in new ones, that it frees the scholar from chance and is a 'democratic' method, and the question of whether a completeness axiom can be added to a system of axioms. |
| 324 | 600_3 | 133 | [130] | question | left open | ist das Dreikörperproblem gelöst oder nicht? | Is the three-body problem solved or not? | Hilbert contrasts proofs that use the Archimedean axiom, where the number of axiom applications can depend on the data, with non-Archimedean proofs that need a fixed number of steps, and then proposes finding a general method for stating problems precisely so that one can always decide whether they are solved. |
| 325 | 600_3 | 134 | 131 | question | left open | Inwiefern verbürgt mein Vollständigkeitsaxiom die eindeutige [?] deutige Bestimmtheit der Begriffe (des System aller Zahlen, der Geometrie etc.)? | To what extent does my completeness axiom guarantee the unique determinateness of the concepts (of the system of all numbers, of geometry, etc.)? | Miscellaneous notes: improbable states in molecular physics, an argument against theologians that logical definition rather than etymology matters, whether a complete axiom system determines its concepts uniquely (categoricity), and Kronecker's program as an axiomatic demand for a non-Archimedean theory of equations. |

| 328 | 600_3 | 139 | [136] | question | left open | Kennt die Gartenfrau besser, was eine Ebene ist, weil sie sicherer planiren kann oder was eine Gerade ist, weil sie die Wege auf dem Gemüsebeet exakt[?] abstecken kann oder was stetig gekrümmt ist, weil sie die geschwungenen Weglinien sicherer macht? | Does the garden woman know better what a plane is because she can level more surely, or what a straight line is because she can stake out the paths on the vegetable bed exactly, or what is continuously curved because she makes the curved path lines more surely? | Loose reflections: a soldier's respect for a commander who shoots well, the inessential nature of words compared with the thought as 'invariant', whether practical activities such as gardening give better knowledge of geometric concepts, how interests in the same object differ, and whether geometric drawing trains geometric intuition. |

| 592 | 600_3 | 90 | 87 | plan | left open | Den Riemannschen Satz[?] über die Nullstellen der $\zeta(s)$- Funktion und überhaupt [-einen-] neuen Beweisen für die Thatsache, dass die Anzahl der Primzahlen dividirt durch $\frac{l x}{x}$ im Limes $x=\infty$ gleich 1 wird, suche dadurch zu gewinnen, dass ich[?] die die Tchebicheffsche Gleichung $\vartheta(x) + \vartheta(\frac{x}{2}) + \vartheta(\frac{x}{3}) + \cdots \qquad = l[x!]$ systematisch | I seek to obtain Riemann's theorem on the zeros of the zeta(s) function, and in general new proofs of the fact that the number of primes divided by (log x)/x tends to 1 as x goes to infinity, by trying to solve systematically the Chebyshev equation theta(x) + theta(x/2) + theta(x/3) + ... = log[x!] and to work out its theory. | The page finishes an argument that every form near a form expressible as a sum of 2n-th powers of linear forms with positive coefficients is itself so expressible, then sets out a plan to prove the prime number theorem by systematically solving Chebyshev's functional equation, and begins a remark on problems such as quartic surfaces with 11 ovals. |

| 594 | 600_3 | 91 | [88] | plan | left open | Zuerst die ab- stossende Wirkung des elektrisirten Gases als äusse- re Kraft ansetzen und dadurch die Dichtenvertei- lung im Gase bestimmen. | First, treat the repulsive effect of the electrified gas as an external force, and use this to determine the density distribution in the gas. | The page notes that certain decision questions about curves are answerable in principle yet as impractical as computing a remote digit of π^π, then gives a moral maxim, three mathematical trick problems with answers, and a plan for a theory of electrical conduction through gases. |

| 596 | 600_3 | 93 | [90] | plan | left open | Studire die [?] Stosskräfte in der Mie-Bornschen Elektro- | Study the collision forces in the Mie–Born electro[dynamics]. | Statistical mechanics: deriving the most probable velocity distribution of molecules via Lagrange multipliers, then asking for the most probable distribution of an ensemble of systems over distributions, with an analogy to drawing black and white grains of sand, and a closing plan to study collision forces in Mie–Born electrodynamics. |
| 597 | 600_3 | 96 | 93 | plan | left open | Beweise das Zermelo'sche Auswahlprinzip streng — ebenso streng wie den Fundamentalsatz*) der Algebra, indem ich zeige, dass er [-wieder-] nie auf einen Widerspruch führen kann | Prove Zermelo's choice principle rigorously — just as rigorously as the fundamental theorem of algebra — by showing that it can never lead to a contradiction. | Philosophical notes on the Kantian antinomies as calculation errors in the logic calculus, three properties of thought, consistency of axioms within their own domain, axiomatizability as the criterion of science, and a plan to prove Zermelo's axiom of choice consistent. |
| 598 | 600_3 | 97 | [94] | plan | left open | Kammschintegration[?] von Differential-, Inte- gral- Funktionalgl. im unendlichen Ansatz. | Kammsch[?]-integration of differential, integral, and functional equations in the infinite setting. | Hilbert criticises those who ignore experimental physics, characterises mathematics as pure thinking, prophesies a flowering of analysis through its connection with physics (especially statistical mechanics and integration of infinitely many differential equations), and states a heuristic principle for mathematical physics. |
| 599 | 600_3 | 100 | 97 | plan | left open | Versuche auch Logik mittels physikalisch mechanischer Ueberlegungen zu be- gründen, indem durch das Denken mechanische Berührungen ausgelöst werden | Try also to ground logic by means of physical-mechanical considerations, in that mechanical contacts are triggered by thinking. | Hilbert argues that all sciences that produce lasting results ultimately supply material for mathematics, citing Einstein's 1912 letter to Sommerfeld, and proposes grounding logic in physical-mechanical considerations as an invariant kind of 'happening'; he then begins a thesis on applying the laws of probability to logic and mathematical statements. |
| 600 | 600_3 | 108 | 105 | plan | left open | Nunmehr muss man bis auf die Axiome [-A-], bis auf die Logik zurückgehen. | Now one must go back to the axioms, back to logic. | Aphoristic remarks on mathematics needing to turn outward, the axiomatization of physics, physicists' approximations as rigorous limit processes, and Hilbert's shift from taking the integers as given to founding mathematics on axioms and logic after finding the set of all sets. |
| 601 | 600_3 | 113 | [110] | plan | left open | nun müssen wir auch [-...-] wirklich über die Abhänge hinüberkommen auf dem Seile, ohne schwindlich zu werden. | Now we must also really get across the abysses on the rope without becoming dizzy. | Hilbert reflects that real mathematical ideas reduce infinitely many processes to finitely many, illustrating this with closed-form sums and series, and remarks that after Weierstrass's critical school mathematicians must now cross the abysses without getting dizzy. |
| 602 | 600_3 | 118 | 115 | plan | left open | Meine Geometrie als Beispiel nehmen zähle auf, was dabei Alles nur Sprache Kunst[?] und wie er[?] neu und übersichtlich geordnet erscheint. | Take my geometry as an example; enumerate what in it is all merely language/art and how it appears newly and clearly ordered. | Philosophical reflections on mathematical work: criticism versus systematising, Klein's applied versus Hilbert's ideal aims, why problems from physics seem richer and more intuitive (with an example from memory feats), layers of axioms, and the axiomatic method as the method of the future. |
| 603 | 600_3 | 120 | 117 | plan | left open | Sammle alles (denn jeder Gedanke, jede Pointe findet doch irgend wo einmal eine Verwendung) [-Auf-] suche die Analogien auf und gruppire nach Methoden! | Collect everything (for every thought, every point eventually finds a use somewhere), seek out the analogies and group according to methods! | Methodological aphorisms on problem solving, creativity versus criticism, a proposed problem of choosing a distinguished point from an arbitrary point set, the role of the Archimedean axiom in geometry and physics, the group concept, and classifying ideas by method. |
| 604 | 600_3 | 122 | 119 | plan | left open | Ich will zeigen, dass er gewiss ein Königsweg zur math. Physik ist. | I want to show that it is certainly a royal road to mathematical physics. | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: that there is no 'ignorabimus' for the mathematician, that most theorems remain undiscovered, that nature's apparent inexactness reflects the limits of understanding, that mathematics provides logical schemata applicable to different phenomena, and that the axiomatic method is a royal road in mathematics and to mathematical physics. |
| 605 | 600_3 | 122 | 119 | plan | left open | Wo der Physiker sagt, dies Gesetz gilt nur angenähert, präparire den strengen Grenzprocess heraus! | Where the physicist says this law holds only approximately, extract the rigorous limiting process! | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: that there is no 'ignorabimus' for the mathematician, that most theorems remain undiscovered, that nature's apparent inexactness reflects the limits of understanding, that mathematics provides logical schemata applicable to different phenomena, and that the axiomatic method is a royal road in mathematics and to mathematical physics. |

| 607 | 600_3 | 133 | [130] | plan | left open | Stelle eine allgemeine Methode auf, nach der sich ein jedes Problem bestimmt formuliren lässt und sich dann auch stets feststellen lässt, ob ein Problem gelöst ist oder nicht | Set up a general method by which every problem can be formulated definitely, and by which one can then always determine whether a problem is solved or not. | Hilbert contrasts proofs that use the Archimedean axiom, where the number of axiom applications can depend on the data, with non-Archimedean proofs that need a fixed number of steps, and then proposes finding a general method for stating problems precisely so that one can always decide whether they are solved. |

| 615 | 600_1 | 15 | [6] | claim | left open | $(x+\cdots)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}$ läßt sich durch eine Transformation von der Gestalt $x' = \lambda x + \mu x^2 + \cdots$ auf die Form $x'\frac{\partial f}{\partial x'}$ bringen $(\lambda \neq 0)$ | (x+…)∂f/∂x can be brought by a transformation of the form x' = λx + μx^2 + … into the form x'∂f/∂x' (λ ≠ 0). | Miscellaneous short notes: a generalisation of ratio-type convergence criteria for series, a recursion for a number in Hilbert's note on discriminant surfaces, a note on Cayley's problem, the parameter bound for intransitive projective groups, and normal forms of vector fields under analytic coordinate changes. |

| 617 | 600_1 | 15 | [6] | claim | left open | Keine intransitive projektive Gruppe des $R_n$ enthält mehr als $n^2-1$ Parameter | No intransitive projective group of R_n contains more than n^2−1 parameters. | Miscellaneous short notes: a generalisation of ratio-type convergence criteria for series, a recursion for a number in Hilbert's note on discriminant surfaces, a note on Cayley's problem, the parameter bound for intransitive projective groups, and normal forms of vector fields under analytic coordinate changes. |
| 618 | 600_1 | 16 | 7 | claim | left open | Im $R_{2n}$ giebt es nicht mehr als $n(n+1)$ paarweise [-ver-] vertauschbare infinitesimale projektive Transformationen und im $R_{2n+1}$ giebt es nicht mehr als $(n+1)^2$ solche. | In $R_{2n}$ there are no more than $n(n+1)$ pairwise commuting infinitesimal projective transformations, and in $R_{2n+1}$ there are no more than $(n+1)^2$ such. | Miscellaneous notes: a bound on the number of pairwise commuting infinitesimal projective transformations, a question on real roots of the hypergeometric function, a scheme for a space curve, a decision problem for integer solutions of Diophantine equations, and literature for a course on descriptive geometry. |
| 619 | 600_1 | 22 | 13 | claim | left open | Auch unendlich viele Formen haben nur ein endliches System von Invarianten. | Even infinitely many forms have only a finite system of invariants. | Notes on invariant theory: determining which functions are entire, finding systems of ternary linear forms analogous to Gordan's systems of quadratic forms, finding all one-to-one transformations of space, applying the transfer process to a covariant expression, and a remark that even infinitely many forms have a finite system of invariants. |
| 620 | 600_1 | 39 | [30] | claim | left open | Die Ausnahmen werden dann leicht überwältigt durch Grenz- übergang, indem man bloss zu zeigen braucht, dass die genügende Zahl von Bedingungen sich ergiebt. | The exceptions are then easily overcome by passage to the limit, since one merely needs to show that the sufficient number of conditions results. | An argument by continuous variation of coefficients that if no plane meets an intersection curve in the full number of distinct points then no surface of order μ does either, followed by a sketch of how to prove that surfaces through the common points of 4 surfaces are, from some order on, linear combinations of them. |
| 621 | 600_1 | 42 | 33 | claim | left open | Mein Beweis für die Endlichkeit der Invarianten der binären Formen giebt mehr als der Gordansche Beweis: denn er zeigt sich zugleich, dass alle Invariante ganze ganz- zahlige Functionen einer endlichen Zahl von Invarian- ten sind | My proof of the finiteness of the invariants of binary forms gives more than Gordan's proof: for it shows at the same time that all invariants are integral functions with integer coefficients of a finite number of invariants. | A remark that Hilbert's finiteness proof for binary invariants shows integral generation, followed by the task of determining subgroups of the linear group with coefficients linear in parameters, illustrated by composing binary and ternary orthogonal substitutions written in parameters. |
| 622 | 600_1 | 45 | [36] | claim | left open | Mittelst[?] der Reihe $\sum \ln x^n$ kann man $\lim \sum_{\varepsilon=0} \frac{1}{p(1+\varepsilon p)}$ berechnen, d.h. angeben wie dieser limes unendlich wird. | By means of the series $\sum \ln x^n$ one can compute $\lim \sum_{\varepsilon=0} \frac{1}{p(1+\varepsilon p)}$, i.e. state how this limit becomes infinite. | Methodological remarks on formulas and calculation as means versus ends, followed by notes on prime number literature, the limit behaviour of a sum related to the series $\sum \ln x^n$, and a plan to rework Dirichlet's proof of infinitely many primes in arithmetic progressions. |
| 623 | 600_1 | 46 | 37 | claim | left open | Ich behaupte: Jede solche Entscheidung ist durch eine endliche Zahl von Operationen (Reihenoperationen) möglich. | I claim: every such decision is possible by a finite number of operations (sequence operations). | After notes on simplifying a proof (using a definite-integral formula or Schlömilch's Compendium), Hilbert formulates every mathematical problem as deciding, by finitely many operations, whether a rule-generated 0-1 sequence ever contains a 0, and claims that every mathematical problem is solvable. |
| 624 | 600_1 | 46 | 37 | claim | left open | Jedes math. Problem ist lösbar. Alles dem menschlichen Verstande erreichbare (durch reines Denken ohne Materie) ist auch aufzulösen. | Every mathematical problem is solvable. Everything attainable by the human understanding (through pure thought without matter) can also be solved. | After notes on simplifying a proof (using a definite-integral formula or Schlömilch's Compendium), Hilbert formulates every mathematical problem as deciding, by finitely many operations, whether a rule-generated 0-1 sequence ever contains a 0, and claims that every mathematical problem is solvable. |
| 625 | 600_1 | 49 | [40] | claim | left open | Das wäre ein grosser Fortschritt, da man so die ganze Kummersche Theorie der idealen Zahlen (vornehmlich den [-Zahl-] Satz von der Eindeutigkeit der Zerlegung in ideale Primzahlen) mit den gewöhnlichen in der Theorie der ganzen [-rat-] Functionen mod. $p$ [-bewältigen-] angewandten Mitteln bewältigen könnte. | That would be great progress, since one could thereby master the whole Kummer theory of ideal numbers (especially the theorem on the uniqueness of decomposition into ideal primes) with the usual means applied in the theory of integral functions mod p. | Hilbert finishes a remark on the correct criterion for a definition, then plans to extend the principle of continued identities to permutation groups and to Kummer's theory of ideal numbers, and begins a remark comparing mathematicians to miners. |
| 626 | 600_1 | 54 | 45 | claim | left open | Wenn der Modul $(F_1 + \varepsilon G_1, F_2 + \varepsilon G_2, \dots, F_m + \varepsilon G_m)$ für beliebig kleine $\varepsilon$ einen Teiler $N^{\text{ter}}$ Ordnung hat, so hat er für $\varepsilon = 0$ jeden- falls einen Teiler, dessen Ordnung nicht kleiner als $N$ ist. | If the module (F_1 + εG_1, F_2 + εG_2, ..., F_m + εG_m) has a divisor of order N for arbitrarily small ε, then for ε = 0 it certainly has a divisor whose order is not less than N. | A remark that mathematicians should simplify rather than generalize, followed by a proof that an invariant's multiplier is a power of the substitution determinant, a statement on divisors of modules under perturbation, and a statement with proof sketch on the finiteness of invariants of a single linear transformation. |
| 627 | 600_1 | 56 | 47 | claim | left open | Dann kann man aus dieser linearen Invariante leicht alle Invarianten bilden und ihre Endlichkeit erkennen. | Then from this linear invariant one can easily form all invariants and recognize their finiteness. | A sketch of how to obtain the invariants of a transformation group by expanding the transformation coefficients in power series of parameters, reducing to infinitesimal linear transformations, and seeking a linear invariant from which all invariants and their finiteness would follow. |
| 628 | 600_1 | 57 | [48] | claim | left open | 2.) Ueber Potenzdarstellungen (z.B. ternäre Form 5ter Ordg = Summe von 7 5ten Potenzen, Trägheitsgesetz für Formen von höheren Ordnungen) | 2.) On representations by powers (e.g. ternary form of 5th order = sum of 7 fifth powers, law of inertia for forms of higher orders). | Notes on applying Hilbert's termination procedure from his paper on algebraic forms to linear Diophantine equations, a list of usable topics from old manuscripts, a remark on prejudice in mathematical research, and two criticisms of Kronecker's theory of algebraic functions. |
| 629 | 600_1 | 69 | [60] | claim | left open | dass auch wenn bloss die Rechnungs- gesetze der Quaternionen gelten, lassen sich die [-...-] [-...-] Einheiten immer durch wirkliche Substi- tutionen ersetzen. | that even if merely the arithmetic laws of the quaternions hold, the units can always be replaced by actual substitutions. | Research notes proposing to generalise Dedekind's 1885 investigations on complex numbers (hypercomplex systems) to infinitely many units and to quaternions, and to reduce Sylvester's residue theorem, Cayley's intersection theorem and the Riemann-Roch theorem to relations between characteristic functions. |
| 630 | 600_1 | 77 | [68] | claim | left open | Man kann die Theorie d. algebr. Funktionen einer Veränderli- chen mit Vermeidung der Cramer'schen[?] Regel – welche nur die formale Ausführung ist – allein aus der Riemann- schen Fläche (der Cyklen[?]) und den elementaren Sätzen über Potenzreihen ableiten. | One can derive the theory of algebraic functions of one variable, avoiding Cramer's rule (which is only the formal execution), solely from the Riemann surface (the cycles) and the elementary theorems on power series. | Notes on deriving the theory of algebraic functions from the Riemann surface and power series, a methodological maxim about doubting everything learned, a remark that genius is industry and character, and the observation that the theorem that every equation has a group holds only for equations with nonzero discriminant. |
| 631 | 600_1 | 81 | [72] | claim | left open | Die Mathematik ist die einzige Wissenschaft, bei welcher wir nicht ignorabimus sondern im Gegentheil im weitersten Umfang in Bezug auf alle und noch so schwierigen Probleme sagen müssen: noscemus. | Mathematics is the only science in which we must say not 'ignorabimus' (we shall not know) but, on the contrary, to the widest extent and with regard to all problems, however difficult: 'noscemus' (we shall know). | The page ends an argument that certain simultaneously transformed expressions cannot all vanish identically, because a determinant equal to the product of differences would then be zero. It then states that mathematics is the one science where we must say 'noscemus' rather than 'ignorabimus', and begins a definition of a 'system' of numbers or functions. |
| 632 | 600_1 | 90 | 81 | claim | left open | bilden in ihrer Gesamtheit einen [-Rationalitätsbereich-] Integritätsbereich, da Summe und Produkt zweier solcher Ausdrücke wiederum von derselben Form sind, ist eine Idealtheorie nothwendig u. möglich. (v. Hurwitz) | [Expressions a_1 b_1^x + a_2 b_2^x + ..., where a, b, ... are say integers,] form in their totality an integral domain; since sum and product of two such expressions are again of the same form, an ideal theory is necessary and possible. (from Hurwitz) | Notes on proving that sin(1/x) can be expanded in a Fourier series, on an integral domain of expressions a_1 b_1^x + a_2 b_2^x + ... (after Hurwitz), on Gauss's early idea of non-Euclidean space, and on Engel's problem of finding a function whose differential equation flow yields a given power series map. |
| 633 | 600_1 | 134 | 125 | claim | left open | Nach diesen An= gaben wird man wohl den Urbereich, d. h. den so= genannten reducirten Raum für quadratische Formen sowie auch die erzeugenden Substitutionen der Gruppe genau nach der Poincaréschen Methode | According to these indications, one will presumably [obtain] the fundamental domain, i.e. the so-called reduced space for quadratic forms, as well as the generating substitutions of the group, exactly by Poincaré's method. | The page classifies linear substitutions that carry a quadratic form into itself (elliptic/finite, parabolic, and the case on the discriminant locus U=0), and says the reduced space and generating substitutions can be obtained by Poincaré's method. |
| 634 | 600_2 | 19 | [14] | claim | left open | Jeder Satz in der Mathematik kann positiv gefasst und als Existenzsatz von folgender Ge- stalt ausgesprochen werden: Zu einem System von Zahlen von der und der Eigenschaft lässt sich ein System von Zahlen von der und der Eigenschaft zuordnen. | Every theorem in mathematics can be framed positively and expressed as an existence theorem of the following form: to a system of numbers with such-and-such a property one can assign a system of numbers with such-and-such a property. | The page finishes a remark about a generalization filling a gap, claims that every mathematical theorem can be phrased positively as an existence theorem about assigning number systems, and begins a plan to attack Fermat's Last Theorem by studying the function field of $y^\lambda + x^\lambda + 1$ number-theoretically. |
| 635 | 600_2 | 28 | 23 | claim | left open | Alle sol- che[-s-] Fundamentalreihen stellen dann nothwendig ein und denselben Werth dar, wie man leicht zeigen kann, und dieser Werth ist $=f(a)$. | All such fundamental sequences then necessarily represent one and the same value, as one can easily show, and this value is $=f(a)$. | The page completes a criterion for continuity via fundamental sequences, then notes research problems on algebraic numbers satisfying linear differential equations, on proving the rationality of Bernoulli numbers from Riemann's zeta integral, and on proving Wiener's assertion about the special Pascal theorem. |
| 636 | 600_2 | 40 | 35 | claim | left open | Hurwitz braucht zu seinem Beweise für die Zerle- gung in Primideale den folgenden Hilfssatz: Wird $(a_0 x^n + \ldots)(b_0 x^m + \ldots) = c_0 x^{n+m} + \ldots)$ gesetzt, [-so-] und sind $a, b$ irgend welche der links stehenden Coefficienten so genügt $ab$ einer Glei- chung von der Gestalt $(ab)^{\nu} + G_1 (ab)^{\nu-1} + G_2 (ab)^{\nu-2} + \ldots = 0$ | For his proof of the decomposition into prime ideals Hurwitz needs the following lemma: if (a_0 x^n + ...)(b_0 x^m + ...) = c_0 x^{n+m} + ... is set, and a, b are any of the coefficients on the left, then ab satisfies an equation of the form (ab)^ν + G_1 (ab)^{ν-1} + G_2 (ab)^{ν-2} + ... = 0. | Congruences modulo a prime p and an irreducible polynomial P(x), roots of prime functions over such a modulus, and a lemma on products of polynomials used by Hurwitz in his proof of prime-ideal factorization. |
| 637 | 600_2 | 44 | 39 | claim | left open | Übrigens kann man den Beweis auch mittelst der Kronecker'schen Fundamentalgl. führen, vergl. Grundzüge S. 111 oben und S. 115 sowie Hensel, Crelle Bd. 113 S. 74-75. | Incidentally, the proof can also be carried out by means of Kronecker's fundamental equation; compare Grundzüge p. 111 top and p. 115, as well as Hensel, Crelle vol. 113 pp. 74-75. | Hilbert gives a short ideal-theoretic proof of a theorem of Königsberger (from his 1894 Berlin report) on the degree of the number field generated by a root, and then begins a statement about the class number of genus-0 algebraic function fields. |
| 638 | 600_2 | 50 | 45 | claim | left open | Es kommt also nur darauf an zu zeigen dass $\lim_{n=\infty} \frac{f_n(z)}{f_n(z)-(f_n(z))} = 0$ oder $\lim_{n=\infty} \frac{f_n}{(f_n)} = 0$ wird | So it only remains to show that $\lim f_n/(f_n - (f_n)) = 0$ or $\lim f_n/(f_n) = 0$. | Continued-fraction approximation of $(1-z)^\alpha$ via a differential equation built from the three functions $f(z)$, $N(1-z)^\alpha$, $Z$, with a convergence argument by analytic continuation around $z=1$ (Riemann's method), and a note proposing to show e.g. $2^{\sqrt2}$ irrational. |
| 639 | 600_2 | 55 | [50] | claim | left open | Die Zahlen mit Minimum Darstellung sind alle von der Form $2^\nu p$. | The numbers with the minimum number of representations are all of the form $2^\nu p$. | The page gives literature references on orthogonal substitution coefficients and on functions with periodic continued fraction expansions, a remark on Cantor's table of representations of even numbers as sums of two primes with an averaging estimate, and the idea of mappings between n-dimensional regions governed by linear PDEs as a generalisation of conformal mapping. |
| 640 | 600_2 | 59 | [54] | claim | left open | Es gilt nun der Satz $D(y,x) = D(x,y)$. | Now the theorem D(y,x) = D(x,y) holds. | The page discusses discriminants of algebraic fields (Körper) and of an algebraic 'Bund', the claimed theorem D(y,x)=D(x,y), a note doubting these theorems, and the start of a generalisation of Picard's theorems. |
| 641 | 600_2 | 64 | 59 | claim | left open | so Arithmetik, Functionentheorie, Geometrie finden ihre Vereinigung in der Theorie derjenigen Functionen, welche den elliptischen für mehr Veränderliche entsprechen. | so arithmetic, function theory and geometry find their union in the theory of those functions which correspond to the elliptic ones for several variables. | The page ends a bound on the maximum of a function on the interval -1 to +1, compares the union of arithmetic, function theory and geometry in the theory of multi-variable analogues of elliptic functions to the ingredients of gunpowder, and begins a generalization of Cauchy's theorem to two complex variables on an algebraic surface. |
| 642 | 600_2 | 71 | [66] | claim | left open | Es giebt nur eine endliche Anzahl reducirter Formen mit geg. Discriminante bez. in einem gegebenen Körper | There is only a finite number of reduced forms with given discriminant, respectively in a given field. | The page ends a remark on mathematics as an autonomous, unified discipline, defines reduced quadratic and decomposable forms with a finiteness statement, and begins a counterexample for relative degree 2 to a statement about ideals of a base field becoming principal in an unramified relative Abelian extension. |
| 643 | 600_2 | 76 | 71 | claim | left open | Die Endlichkeit der [-A-] Anzahl der Idealklassen für einen Körper algebra- ischer Funktionen 2er Veränderlicher würde [-a-] aussagen, dass es [-eine-] 1.) Eine Schaar von Punkt{+systeme+}[-gruppen-] 2.) eine Schaar von Curven auf der Fläche $f=0$ giebt, so dass alle [-at-] anderen Punktsysteme und jede andere Curve auf $f=0$ einem Individuum jener Schaar aequivalent ist. | The finiteness of the number of ideal classes for a field of algebraic functions of two variables would state that there exist 1.) a family of point systems and 2.) a family of curves on the surface f=0 such that all other point systems and every other curve on f=0 is equivalent to a member of that family. | Notes on the theory of fields as a theory of the four arithmetic operations (Körper, Feld, Ring, Gitter), a claim that the only one-to-one, continuous, differentiable map of the rationals between 0 and 1 onto themselves is a linear fractional function, and the finiteness of the number of ideal classes for a field of algebraic functions of two variables. |
| 644 | 600_2 | 76 | 71 | claim | left open | jede umkehrbar eindeutige, stetige und differentiirbare Funktion von x, welche [-x-] für jedes rationale x nur und zwar alle rationalen Zahlen annimmt ist $= \frac{ax+b}{cx+d}$ wo a, b, c, d rationale Zahlen be- deuten. | every one-to-one, continuous and differentiable function of x which, for rational x, takes only rational values, and indeed all rational numbers, is equal to (ax+b)/(cx+d), where a, b, c, d denote rational numbers. | Notes on the theory of fields as a theory of the four arithmetic operations (Körper, Feld, Ring, Gitter), a claim that the only one-to-one, continuous, differentiable map of the rationals between 0 and 1 onto themselves is a linear fractional function, and the finiteness of the number of ideal classes for a field of algebraic functions of two variables. |
| 645 | 600_2 | 79 | [74] | claim | left open | Ferner Es[?] lässt sich keine Gl. noch so hohen Grades mit einem Parameter angeben, so dass die Wurzeln der allgemein. Gl. 6 {+ten+} Grades sich durch Wurzelzeichen aus Para- metern und den Wurzeln jener Gl. darstellen lassen. | Furthermore, no equation of however high a degree with one parameter can be given such that the roots of the general equation of degree 6 can be expressed by radicals in terms of parameters and the roots of that equation. | The page sketches how to show, without Riemann's integral, that the ξ-function has genus 1 as a function of t², asks whether the prime factors of Frobenius's group determinant are themselves determinants, and poses a problem on reducing the sextic equation by radicals to one or two parameters. |
| 646 | 600_2 | 80 | 75 | claim | left open | Für algebraische Functionen enthält dieser Satz den Riemann-Roch- 'schen Satz vgl.
