Landsberg, Göttinger Nachrichten. | Notes on page 75 cover a plan to list number fields with small discriminants, a remark on generalised (lemniscatic) powers of Gaussian integers, a reference to Jacobi, and a derivation that the ideal built from the first minors of a basis determinant equals A times the different, with a remark connecting this to the Riemann–Roch theorem. |
| 647 | 600_2 | 80 | 75 | claim | left open | Man muss z. B. beim Studium der Zahlen $a+bi$ auch Potenzen $(a+bi)^{k+ih}$ zulassen, wo dies aber nicht gewöhnliche Potenzen sind (vielmehr Lemniscatenfunctionen.) Doch gilt noch nach einem gewissen Modul $1+i$ dasselbe einfache Additions- theorem wie für die Exponentialfunction | For example, when studying the numbers a+bi one must also admit powers (a+bi)^{k+ih}, where these are not ordinary powers, however (rather lemniscatic functions). Yet, modulo a certain modulus 1+i, the same simple addition theorem still holds as for the exponential function. | Notes on page 75 cover a plan to list number fields with small discriminants, a remark on generalised (lemniscatic) powers of Gaussian integers, a reference to Jacobi, and a derivation that the ideal built from the first minors of a basis determinant equals A times the different, with a remark connecting this to the Riemann–Roch theorem. |
| 648 | 600_2 | 89 | [84] | claim | left open | Eine Gl. 3[-.-]ten oder 4[-.-]ten Grades ist in [-dem-] einem Rationalitätsbereiche stets lösbar, wenn sie [-...-] [-...-] nach jedem Modul als Congruenz aufgefaßt lösbar ist | An equation of 3rd or 4th degree is always solvable in a domain of rationality if it is solvable when regarded as a congruence modulo every modulus. | A pasted-in plan for Hilbert's future number-theoretic studies (relative abelian fields, Fermat's theorem, complex multiplication), followed by notes on solvability of quadratic and higher-degree Diophantine equations, a remark on Molien's paper on hypercomplex numbers, and an elementary proof of the arithmetic–geometric mean inequality after Hurwitz. |
| 649 | 600_2 | 91 | [86] | claim | left open | $x^2+2=y^n$ hat für $n>3$ keine ganzzahlige Lösung | x^2+2=y^n has no integer solution for n>3 | The page ends a note on a geometric distance problem, records claims that x^2+2=y^n and x^2+5=y^n have no integer solutions, and states Minkowski's theorem on convex surfaces with prescribed Gaussian curvature as a function of the normal, with a sketch of the plane case. |
| 650 | 600_2 | 91 | [86] | claim | left open | $x^2+5=y^n$ " überhaupt keine " " | x^2+5=y^n has no integer solution at all | The page ends a note on a geometric distance problem, records claims that x^2+2=y^n and x^2+5=y^n have no integer solutions, and states Minkowski's theorem on convex surfaces with prescribed Gaussian curvature as a function of the normal, with a sketch of the plane case. |
| 651 | 600_2 | 101 | [96] | claim | left open | Der Satz von der Wurzelexistenz lässt sich rein geometrisch völlig streng beweisen. | The theorem on the existence of roots can be proved purely geometrically and completely rigorously. | Brief philosophical remarks: a definition of mathematics, the claim that geometry and arithmetic are fundamentally the same, and the remark that number theory and function theory are united in class field theory (complex multiplication). |
| 652 | 600_2 | 101 | [96] | claim | left open | Die Geometrie lässt sich völlig strenge auf- bauen. | Geometry can be built up completely rigorously. | Brief philosophical remarks: a definition of mathematics, the claim that geometry and arithmetic are fundamentally the same, and the remark that number theory and function theory are united in class field theory (complex multiplication). |
| 653 | 600_3 | 5 | [2] | claim | left open | Hülfssatz 3 Die [-Gl-] Diophantische Gleichung $\nu\xi^2+\mu\eta^2=1$ ist in [-allen-] Zahlen $\xi,\eta$ des Körpers $k(\nu,\mu)$ stets lösbar, wenn für alle Primideale $\mathfrak{w}$ in $k(\nu,\mu)$ stets $\left(\frac{\nu,\mu}{\mathfrak{w}}\right)=+1$ ausfällt und keine der conjugirten Gleichungen mit reellen $\nu,\mu$ definit ist. | Auxiliary lemma 3: The Diophantine equation $\nu\xi^2+\mu\eta^2=1$ is always solvable in numbers $\xi,\eta$ of the field $k(\nu,\mu)$ if for all prime ideals $\mathfrak{w}$ in $k(\nu,\mu)$ one always has $(\nu,\mu / \mathfrak{w})=+1$ and none of the conjugate equations with real $\nu,\mu$ is definite. | Hilbert completes a counting argument on quadratic residues modulo powers of a prime ideal l, states an auxiliary lemma (Hülfssatz 3) on the solvability of $\nu\xi^2+\mu\eta^2=1$ in the field $k(\nu,\mu)$, and sets up three simultaneous Diophantine equations to prove the main theorem. |
| 654 | 600_3 | 5 | [2] | claim | left open | und beweisen, dass diese 3 Gleichungen simultan durch Zahlen $\xi_1\eta_1,\xi_2\eta_2,\xi_3\eta_3$ des Körpers $k(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)=k$ lösen kann wenn man für $\pi,\kappa$ geeignete zu 2 prime ganze Zahlen in $k$ wählt. | and prove that these 3 equations can be solved simultaneously by numbers $\xi_1\eta_1,\xi_2\eta_2,\xi_3\eta_3$ of the field $k(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)=k$ if one chooses suitable integers $\pi,\kappa$ in $k$ that are prime to 2. | Hilbert completes a counting argument on quadratic residues modulo powers of a prime ideal l, states an auxiliary lemma (Hülfssatz 3) on the solvability of $\nu\xi^2+\mu\eta^2=1$ in the field $k(\nu,\mu)$, and sets up three simultaneous Diophantine equations to prove the main theorem. |
| 655 | 600_3 | 16 | 13 | claim | left open | Dies ist jedoch wahrscheinlich sehr schwer zu beweisen. | This, however, is probably very hard to prove. | Hilbert discusses which functions of three variables can be built by successive composition of functions of two arguments, and proposes the root t of t^6 + x t^3 + y t^2 + z t + 1 = 0 as probably the simplest example of a genuine function of three variables, noting that a proof would likely be very hard. |
| 656 | 600_3 | 27 | 24 | claim | left open | 2.) zeigt den Weg, wie diese Voraussetzung[?] [-wird bewiesen-] bewiesen wird[?] wenn auch nur bisher nicht gelungen. | 2.) shows the way in which this assumption [?] is to be proved, even if this has not yet succeeded so far. | Notes on the foundations of mathematics, comparing the arithmetic of irrational numbers (Dedekind, Weierstrass, Cantor) with Euclidean geometry, and weighing two approaches: the geometric one and the analytic or genetic definition. |
| 657 | 600_3 | 28 | 25 | claim | left open | Es kann doch bei gegebenen Voraussetzungen nur einen einfachsten Beweis geben. | Under given assumptions there can surely be only one simplest proof. | Hilbert notes a plan to analyse linkage constructions for the simplest apparatus, then describes the problem he had wanted to pose as the 24th problem of his Paris lecture: criteria for the simplicity of proofs and a theory of proof methods, with syzygies as an example. |
| 658 | 600_3 | 29 | [26] | claim | left open | so kann man diese zählen und z. B. beim Beweis bestimmter Sätze in der [-x-] Elementargeometrie (Pythagoras, oder über merkwürdige Punkte im Dreieck) sehr wohl entscheiden, welcher der einfachste Beweis ist. | so one can count these and, for example, in proving certain theorems of elementary geometry (Pythagoras, or on remarkable points in the triangle), decide quite well which is the simplest proof. | Remarks on comparing the simplicity of proofs by counting elementary steps, a programmatic note on turning differential geometry and physics into 'integral' versions, and a theorem that continuous functions agreeing in all iterated integrals over [0,1] are identical, proved via expansion in spherical (Legendre) functions. |
| 659 | 600_3 | 33 | [30] | claim | left open | Auf jeder geschlossenen im Endlichen gelegenen [-Fläche-] sin- gularitätenfreien Fläche giebt es mindestens 2 geschlossene d.h. in sich zurückkehrende geodätische Linien. | On every closed, singularity-free surface lying in the finite there are at least 2 closed, i.e. self-returning, geodesic lines. | Notes on how to determine general integrals of partial differential equations by boundary data on non-characteristic curves, a conjecture that such solutions depend algebraically on a linearly occurring parameter, and a claim about closed geodesics on closed surfaces illustrated by Finsterwalder's lamella model. |
| 660 | 600_3 | 33 | [30] | claim | left open | Sind diese gleich lang, so giebt es jedenfalls durch jeden Punkt der Fläche eine geschlossene Linie | If these are of equal length, then in any case there is a closed line through every point of the surface. | Notes on how to determine general integrals of partial differential equations by boundary data on non-characteristic curves, a conjecture that such solutions depend algebraically on a linearly occurring parameter, and a claim about closed geodesics on closed surfaces illustrated by Finsterwalder's lamella model. |
| 661 | 600_3 | 35 | [32] | claim | left open | würde die Sachlage so auffassen, dass die hyperbolische Diff. doppelt soviel Integrale habe als die elliptische | [I] would view the situation such that the hyperbolic differential [equation] has twice as many integrals as the elliptic one. | Notes for Blumenthal's habilitation colloquium on arbitrary functions in the general integrals of partial differential equations, analytic versus non-analytic solutions (elliptic vs. hyperbolic equations), uniqueness of analytic continuation, and a remark that productive researchers should look to border areas between fields. |
| 662 | 600_3 | 43 | [40] | claim | left open | Alle meine Geometrien und die aus[?] denselben folgenden Resultate laufen auf Nicht-Archimedische Gruppen hinaus. | All my geometries and the results following from them come down to non-Archimedean groups. | The page argues that a Dedekind cut need not exist in a non-Archimedean number system, states that his geometries lead to non-Archimedean groups, and attempts a construction of a line parallel to two given lines in Lobachevskian geometry; that last section is crossed out. |
| 663 | 600_3 | 46 | 43 | claim | left open | diese ganze Idee offenbar grosser Verallgemeinerung fähig ist | this whole idea is obviously capable of great generalisation | Hilbert derives conditions on polynomials P for which the integral of 1/((x^2-1)P^2) has no logarithmic terms, links them to the denominators of continued-fraction convergents, and plans generalisations: simultaneous continued fractions, approximation of e^x and Bessel functions with irrationality proofs, and connection with Abel's work on periodic continued fractions. |
| 664 | 600_3 | 48 | 45 | claim | left open | Das Unendlichviel Körper-problem {+(z.B. in der kinetischen Gastheorie)+} ist zweifellos {+viel+} leichter als das 3- Körperproblem zu behandeln. | The infinitely-many-body problem (e.g. in the kinetic theory of gases) is undoubtedly much easier to treat than the three-body problem. | Notes on the existence of rational integrals of differential equations related to Lamé functions, continued fraction expansions connected to the Weierstrass p-function and Bessel-type equations, a remark that the infinitely-many-body problem is easier than the three-body problem, and a question about proving the change of a curve potential via a surface integral. |
| 665 | 600_3 | 48 | 45 | claim | left open | so genügen die Reste einer Differen- tialgl. 3 ter Ordnung, deren Coeffizienten nur etwa vom 2 ten oder 3 ten Grade (Jedenfalls von einem von $n$ unabhängigen Grade sind. | then the remainders satisfy a third-order differential equation whose coefficients are only of about 2nd or 3rd degree (in any case of a degree independent of n). | Notes on the existence of rational integrals of differential equations related to Lamé functions, continued fraction expansions connected to the Weierstrass p-function and Bessel-type equations, a remark that the infinitely-many-body problem is easier than the three-body problem, and a question about proving the change of a curve potential via a surface integral. |
| 666 | 600_3 | 51 | [48] | claim | left open | Hurwitz [?] meint, dass 1.) bei dem Problem der Mondtheorie (Störung durch die Sonne) und 2.) in dem Problem der 2 Traban- ten des Saturn | Hurwitz [?] thinks that 1.) in the problem of lunar theory (perturbation by the sun) and 2.) in the problem of the 2 satellites of Saturn ... mathematical analysis fails, and since Poincaré's methods are of no use while observation has given exact results, experiment has run ahead of theory. | Notes on Hurwitz's [?] remark that analysis fails for the lunar theory and the problem of two Saturn satellites, a plan to transfer the method of variation of constants to partial differential equations, and cosine expansions leading to partial fraction expansions and to the Weierstrass elliptic function. |
| 667 | 600_3 | 56 | 53 | claim | left open | Für $x=0$ würde man das Resultat erhalten, dass $\sqrt{\pi}$ irrational ist. | For $x=0$ one would obtain the result that $\sqrt{\pi}$ is irrational. | Notes on using continued fraction (and ternary chain-algorithm) expansions of the error-type function $e^{x^2}\int_0^x e^{-t^2}dt$ together with $e^{-x^2}$ to prove irrationality results, via differential equations, recursion formulas and generating functions. |
| 668 | 600_3 | 60 | 57 | claim | left open | meine Behauptung von der endlichen Anzahl der Zykeln | my assertion about the finite number of cycles | Notes on branch points and the variational calculus (minimal functions, Lagrange's equation, constructibility of fields), on characteristics of first-order partial differential equations as lines along which solutions stay unbranched, and a plan to characterise multiple Poincaré limit cycles in order to prove that the number of cycles is finite. |
| 669 | 600_3 | 63 | [60] | claim | left open | (dies ist für Unstetigkeitsflächen der Flüssigkeitsbewegung gewiss zutreffend!) | (this is certainly true for surfaces of discontinuity in fluid motion!) | The page discusses minimum principles and stability criteria in statics and dynamics (F+V, F+T, d'Alembert and Hamilton principles), gives an example differential equation of higher degree with its general integral, and notes a plan to connect Cauchy's method for linear PDEs with constant coefficients to his integral equations and apply it to Maxwell's equations. |
| 670 | 600_3 | 63 | [60] | claim | left open | Im übrigen ist die Stabilitäts- Labilitätsfrage eine relative. It depends on what one prescribes or holds fixed in the experiment. | The page discusses minimum principles and stability criteria in statics and dynamics (F+V, F+T, d'Alembert and Hamilton principles), gives an example differential equation of higher degree with its general integral, and notes a plan to connect Cauchy's method for linear PDEs with constant coefficients to his integral equations and apply it to Maxwell's equations. |
| 671 | 600_3 | 64 | 61 | claim | left open | Es könnte [-an-] im Querschnitt [-auch der-] statt der Ge- schwindigkeit der Druck gegeben sein. | In the cross-section, the pressure could also be given instead of the velocity. | Hilbert formulates plane viscous incompressible flow in a channel between the walls y=0 and y=1 as the biharmonic boundary value problem ΔΔφ=0, the same problem as the elasticity of a plate; he then notes an application of his generalized Green's function principle to algebraic functions and the genus, and proposes studying the connection between oscillation theorems. |
| 672 | 600_3 | 64 | 61 | claim | left open | Mein Princip der Greenschen Funktion in weiterem Sinne kann man auf die Theorie der algebraischen Funktionen anwenden: Das Geschlecht $p$ bedeutet dann dass die Differentialgl. $\Delta u+\lambda u=0$ $\lambda=0$ zum $p$ fachen Eigenwert hat, [-was dann-] so dass dann zu $\Delta u=0$ eine $p$ mal erweiterte Greensche Funktion (mit nur einer logarithmi- schen Unendlichkeitsstelle gehört. | My principle of the Green's function in the wider sense can be applied to the theory of algebraic functions: the genus p then means that the differential equation Δu+λu=0 has λ=0 as a p-fold eigenvalue, so that a p-times extended Green's function (with only one logarithmic singularity) then belongs to Δu=0. | Hilbert formulates plane viscous incompressible flow in a channel between the walls y=0 and y=1 as the biharmonic boundary value problem ΔΔφ=0, the same problem as the elasticity of a plate; he then notes an application of his generalized Green's function principle to algebraic functions and the genus, and proposes studying the connection between oscillation theorems. |
| 673 | 600_3 | 70 | 67 | claim | left open | Dann müssen sich die $P^{(\alpha)}([-M-]x)$ als die Eigenfunktionen einer Differentialgleichung ergeben etc. | Then the P^(α)(x) must turn out to be the eigenfunctions of a differential equation, etc. | Hilbert considers orthogonality properties of polynomials P_n with a weight factor (ξ−α), plans to find a second-order differential equation for them, and reduces simultaneous rational approximation of two functions to finding three polynomials P, Q, R. |
| 674 | 600_3 | 71 | [68] | claim | left open | einem Satze, dass jedes willkürliche Paar von Funktio- nen sich simultan nach den Paaren Eigenfunktionen mit denselben Fourier-Koeffizienten entwickeln lässt. | One arrives at a theorem that every arbitrary pair of functions can be expanded simultaneously in the pairs of eigenfunctions with the same Fourier coefficients. | Hilbert discusses a third-order differential equation satisfied by a remainder A in simultaneous rational approximation of two functions φ, ψ, the requirement for an irrationality proof, the orthogonality property of P_n when φ, ψ are Cauchy-type integrals, and simultaneous expansion of pairs of functions in eigenfunctions with the same Fourier coefficients. |
| 675 | 600_3 | 88 | 85 | claim | left open | Man könnte dies durch Abzählen der Koeffizienten der Potenzreihen, wie in meinen früheren Beweisen machen, aber es müsste auch aus der allgl. Ueberlegung über bestimmte Systeme von partiellen Differentialgl. durchführbar sein. | One could do this by counting the coefficients of the power series, as in my earlier proofs, but it should also be feasible from the general consideration of determined systems of partial differential equations. | Hilbert discusses when a system of partial differential equations (including a Jacobian-type determinant condition) is genuinely determined, proposes finding a general criterion for this, and then sketches a plan to prove his independence theorem in the calculus of variations by deriving a partial differential equation for the minimum as a function of the endpoint. |
| 676 | 600_3 | 91 | [88] | claim | left open | Dann muss die Lei- tungskonstante durch meine Theorie heraus- kommen, sowie auch zugleich die Sommerfeld- sche Weglänge. | Then the conduction constant must come out of my theory, and at the same time also Sommerfeld's path length. | The page notes that certain decision questions about curves are answerable in principle yet as impractical as computing a remote digit of π^π, then gives a moral maxim, three mathematical trick problems with answers, and a plan for a theory of electrical conduction through gases. |
| 677 | 600_3 | 92 | 89 | claim | left open | Die Lebensmikroben[?] sind genügend klein, um solche Apparate zu sein, die Wärme in Arbeit zu verwandeln. | The microbes of life are small enough to be such devices that convert heat into work. | Notes on using a mean-free-path-type method to solve an integral equation, on the role of experiment and the axiomatic method in mathematics versus physics, a speculative remark on the second law of thermodynamics and perpetual motion of the second kind, and the start of a statistical (Boltzmann-type) maximization for the distribution of molecular energies. |
| 678 | 600_3 | 94 | 91 | claim | left open | Kronecker war inconsequent; er hätte auch die ganze Zahl erst recht verbieten müssen. | Kronecker was inconsistent; he should all the more have forbidden the integer as well. | The page ends a remark about electrodynamics and relativity theory, then notes that Kronecker was inconsistent and should, by his own logic, have forbidden even the integers. |
| 679 | 600_3 | 96 | 93 | claim | left open | wobei natürlich herauskommen muss, dass er nur gültig ist für die vernünftige, wohl definirte (d.h. durch endliche Anzahl von Axiom[?] festgelegte Menge). | whereby it must of course come out that it is valid only for the reasonable, well-defined set (i.e. one fixed by a finite number of axioms). | Philosophical notes on the Kantian antinomies as calculation errors in the logic calculus, three properties of thought, consistency of axioms within their own domain, axiomatizability as the criterion of science, and a plan to prove Zermelo's axiom of choice consistent. |
| 680 | 600_3 | 97 | [94] | claim | left open | Aus der Verbindung mit Physik, insbesondere der statisti- schen Mechanik prophezeie ich eine Blütezeit der Math. wie sie sie nie zuvor gehabt. | From the connection with physics, in particular statistical mechanics, I prophesy a golden age of mathematics such as it has never had before. | Hilbert criticises those who ignore experimental physics, characterises mathematics as pure thinking, prophesies a flowering of analysis through its connection with physics (especially statistical mechanics and integration of infinitely many differential equations), and states a heuristic principle for mathematical physics. |
| 681 | 600_3 | 97 | [94] | claim | left open | Sobald die Math alle die Grenzprovinzen erobert hat, prophezeie ich auch für die reine Analysis eine Blütezeit, wie sie sie noch nie erlebt hat. | As soon as mathematics has conquered all the border provinces, I prophesy for pure analysis too a golden age such as it has never yet experienced. | Hilbert criticises those who ignore experimental physics, characterises mathematics as pure thinking, prophesies a flowering of analysis through its connection with physics (especially statistical mechanics and integration of infinitely many differential equations), and states a heuristic principle for mathematical physics. |
| 682 | 600_3 | 99 | [96] | claim | left open | Beweis aller Beweise: dass man den Beweis immer muss finden können! | Proof of all proofs: that one must always be able to find the proof! | Philosophical notes on applying statistical principles to mathematical facts, the sameness of the simplest logical propositions, pre-established harmony between thought and being, intellectual refinement versus thermodynamic degradation, monism, the axiomatic method as the general form of thinking, and the 'proof of all proofs'. |
| 683 | 600_3 | 102 | 99 | claim | left open | Wenn $M$ eine Menge von Mengen ist, [-dann Axiome-] {+die+} nicht einen Wi- derspruch einschliesst, so thut sie es auch nicht, wenn wir das Auswahlaxiom hinzufügen. | If M is a set of sets which does not contain a contradiction, then it also does not do so when we add the axiom of choice. | Philosophical and foundational remarks: a chess analogy, Germany's lead in the theory of number from Dedekind and Cantor's dispute with Kronecker, a relative-consistency formulation of Zermelo's axiom of choice, and the relativity of 'working hypothesis' versus 'real existence' with reference to Planck. |
| 684 | 600_3 | 107 | [104] | claim | left open | Dass es kein Ignorabimus in der Mathematik giebt, ist wahrscheinlich durch meine Theorie der Logik-Arithmetik beweisbar | That there is no Ignorabimus in mathematics is probably provable by my theory of logic-arithmetic. | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: its role relative to philosophy, its unity across physics and number theory, a warning against superfluous generalisation, the conjectured provable absence of an 'Ignorabimus', and the relation between theory and empirical data. |
| 685 | 600_3 | 123 | [120] | claim | left open | Specielle {+bloss+} interessante, frappirende, unerwartete Einzelresultate sind oft Anfänge allgemeinerer grösserer Theorien | Special, merely interesting, striking, unexpected individual results are often the beginnings of more general, larger theories. | A page of aphorisms on teaching, mathematical research, the intellect, Kronecker's views, and writing papers. |
| 686 | 600_3 | 123 | [120] | claim | left open | dass die meisten Sätze in der Math. noch unentdeckt sind | that most theorems in mathematics are still undiscovered. | A page of aphorisms on teaching, mathematical research, the intellect, Kronecker's views, and writing papers. |
| 687 | 600_3 | 131 | [128] | claim | left open | Die Constanten würden dann nur durch die Anfangszustände hineinkommen. | The constants would then enter only through the initial states. | Methodological reflections: the lasting value of old theories, an analogy between the continuity axioms of geometry and molecular hypotheses in physics (including the possible elimination of physical constants), and arguments for the axiomatic method. |
| 688 | 600_1 | 118 | 109 | conjecture | unclear | So folgt vielleicht jede | Thus perhaps every ... follows (the sentence continues on the next page) | Hilbert lists properties of systems of forms (closure under addition and subtraction, closure under integral rational functions, divisibility, finite basis), defines a 'full system of forms', and sets out a plan to prove which properties such systems have. |
| 689 | 600_2 | 11 | [6] | conjecture | unclear | und dazu wird wahrscheinlich die folgende Funktion [-....-] $f_0(x) = $ [-....-] [-.... x = \sqrt{2}..-] $\left(x - \sqrt{\frac{x^2+1}{2}}\right)^n$ | and for this purpose the following function will probably serve: f_0(x) = (x - sqrt((x^2+1)/2))^n, with f_1, f_2, ... obtained by differentiating and multiplying by sqrt(x^2+1), f = f_n. | Hilbert considers best approximation of a continuous function by integral functions of the field (x, sqrt(x^2+1)) of the form G(x)+H(x)sqrt(x^2+1), proposes an analogue of Gauss's orthogonality method with a candidate function built by repeated differentiation, and suggests generalizing to arbitrary algebraic function fields and continued fraction expansions. |
| 690 | 600_2 | 13 | [8] | conjecture | unclear | Wurzel vielleicht der Zahl $e^2$, $2\pi$. | Root perhaps of the number $e^2$, $2\pi$. | Hilbert notes that the convergence of the power series of e^{-1/(1-x)} everywhere on the unit circle, shown by Saalschütz through computation, follows from Fourier expansion, and he begins stating a general theorem on convergence on the boundary of the circle of convergence when there are finitely many singular points. |
| 691 | 600_3 | 100 | 97 | conjecture | unclear | Eine Art $\Omega$-These: man kann die Gesetze der Wahrscheinlich- keit auf Logik und mathematische Behauptungen (z.B. Max- | A kind of Ω-thesis: one can [apply] the laws of probability to logic and mathematical statements (e.g. Max- | Hilbert argues that all sciences that produce lasting results ultimately supply material for mathematics, citing Einstein's 1912 letter to Sommerfeld, and proposes grounding logic in physical-mechanical considerations as an invariant kind of 'happening'; he then begins a thesis on applying the laws of probability to logic and mathematical statements. |
| 692 | 600_3 | 149 | 4r | conjecture | unclear | Im Gegensatz zu meiner bisherigen Auffassung scheint es, ist gerade das Gleich ($=$) eine Negation, nämlich die von Verschieden ($\neq$). | Contrary to my previous view, it seems that it is precisely equality (=) that is a negation, namely that of 'different' (≠). | Philosophical notes on mathematics as the foundation of natural laws, the Lorentz transformation as an example of pre-established harmony, axiomatics as a 'super-consciousness', and the idea that equality (=) is the negation of difference (≠). |
| 693 | 600_1 | 10 | 1 | problem | unclear | Schließungsproblem für die vorige Aufgabe | Closure problem for the previous task. | Notes on representing a binary form as a sum of powers of the linear factors of another form, the vanishing of the last transvectant (Überschiebung), linear dependence of forms, and examples with cubic and quartic forms involving the invariants i and j. |

| 695 | 600_1 | 35 | [26] | problem | unclear | Bestimmung der Zahl der Primzahlproducte $pq$ welche $< n$ sind. | Determination of the number of prime products pq which are < n. | The page poses counting the prime products pq below n, gives a remark on the nature of number as the bearer of a rule or a 'wandering cut', and states an axiom for passing from number to geometric magnitude by mapping all numbers one-to-one onto a curve. |

Sought are all integral (polynomial) functions with integer coefficients that remain unchanged. | Three short notes: invariants of a single integral linear transformation reduced via canonical form to simultaneous binary invariants; reducing all algebraic equations to simultaneous quadratic equations, so algebraic surfaces come from quadratic forms; and a proposal to express continued fractions and higher continued-fraction algorithms through solutions of differential equations, as sums are ap |
| 697 | 600_1 | 116 | 107 | problem | unclear | und es entsteht daher die Aufgabe sie auf das kleinste Mass zu reduciren | and therefore the task arises of reducing them to the smallest measure | Hilbert proposes reducing a set of conditions by considering moduli of the form (φ, ψ, (x−a)^λ(y−b)^μ), then remarks that finding all integral rational functions invariant under a single integral linear substitution can be separated from binary invariant theory, and that invariance under infinitely many substitutions essentially yields only semi-invariants. |
| 698 | 600_1 | 122 | 113 | problem | unclear | Beweis dass die 4 Lösungen $f = xy,\ xt,\ yz,$ $zt$, der Differentialgleichung | Proof that the 4 solutions f = xy, xt, yz, zt of the differential equation x ∂f/∂x − y ∂f/∂y + z ∂f/∂z − t ∂f/∂t = 0 form the full system of entire rational solutions. | The page concludes a statement that every function of a function field congruent to zero modulo the system of all entire rational functions of the field is an entire algebraic function of them, and begins a proof that xy, xt, yz, zt form the full system of entire rational solutions of the partial differential equation x f_x − y f_y + z f_z − t f_t = 0. |
| 699 | 600_2 | 4 | 0 | problem | unclear | Beweise, dass die Bernoullische Function irre= ducibel ist. | Prove that the Bernoulli function is irreducible. | Three short notes: proving that the Bernoulli function (polynomial) is irreducible, using its behaviour modulo a prime degree p; a remark that an ideal probability device (wheel of fortune) is impossible; and a task to set up a theory of calculation. |
| 700 | 600_2 | 10 | 5 | problem | unclear | Stelle die Bedingung dafür auf, dass er auch noch für $f(x)=\sin(n+1)x, \cos(n+1)x, \dots$ genau ist. | Set up the condition for it to be exact also for f(x) = sin(n+1)x, cos(n+1)x, ... | A quadrature problem: choosing nodes alpha_i so that an approximation of the integral of f from 0 to 1, exact for trigonometric combinations up to order n, is also exact for sin(n+1)x, cos(n+1)x, ..., by analogy with Gauss and Jacobi quadrature for polynomials. |
| 701 | 600_2 | 11 | [6] | problem | unclear | Wie kann man eine gegebene Funktion am besten durch ein ganze Funktion [-von gegebenem der gege-] des gegebenen Körpers $(x, \sqrt{x^2+1})$ von gegebenem Grade darstellen? | How can one best represent a given function by an integral function of the given field (x, sqrt(x^2+1)) of given degree? | Hilbert considers best approximation of a continuous function by integral functions of the field (x, sqrt(x^2+1)) of the form G(x)+H(x)sqrt(x^2+1), proposes an analogue of Gauss's orthogonality method with a candidate function built by repeated differentiation, and suggests generalizing to arbitrary algebraic function fields and continued fraction expansions. |
| 702 | 600_2 | 18 | 13 | problem | unclear | so bedarf es des Nachweises, dass die gesuch- te das Integral zum Maximum oder Mini- mum machende Function auch noch die höheren Differentialquotienten besitzt. | it requires the proof that the sought function, which makes the integral a maximum or minimum, also possesses the higher derivatives. | Hilbert finishes a proof that an approximating function must be divisible by x^2 and proposes generalizing the result to the calculus of variations, to show that the extremizing function possesses higher derivatives via uniform approximation by rational functions and partial integration. |

| 704 | 600_2 | 28 | 23 | problem | unclear | Beweise die Wienersche Behauptung, (Verhandlun- gen der Gesellschaft deutscher Naturforscher zu Halle. S. 8,) dass der specielle Pascalsche Satz nicht | Prove Wiener's assertion (Proceedings of the Society of German Natural Scientists at Halle, p. 8) that the special Pascal theorem not [continues on next page] | The page completes a criterion for continuity via fundamental sequences, then notes research problems on algebraic numbers satisfying linear differential equations, on proving the rationality of Bernoulli numbers from Riemann's zeta integral, and on proving Wiener's assertion about the special Pascal theorem. |
| 705 | 600_2 | 67 | [62] | problem | unclear | Grundproblem der Reduction besteht darin n lineare Formen | The basic problem of reduction consists in [taking] n linear forms | The page concludes an argument that a function which is rational in y for each x (and conversely) and continuous in x is a rational function of x and y, then begins stating the basic problem of reduction of n linear forms. |
| 706 | 600_2 | 82 | 77 | problem | unclear | Beweise, dass es ein System von Gl. $f_1(x_1 .. x_n) = 0$, $f_2(x_1 .. x_n) = 0$, .. giebt, so dass alle durch die ersten Gl. dargestellten $x_1 .. x_n$ die letzten Gl. befriedigen und auch die letzten Gl. nur durch jene Wertsysteme befriedigt werden. | Prove that there is a system of equations $f_1(x_1..x_n)=0$, $f_2(x_1..x_n)=0$, ..., such that all $x_1..x_n$ represented by the first equations satisfy the latter equations, and the latter equations are satisfied only by those systems of values. | The page ends a remark that function theory draws its strength from its border with number theory and should follow arithmetic, gives an aphorism about the heights of arithmetic, and then sets a problem with a proof sketch: show that points given by homogeneous parametrizations $x_i=\varphi_i(t_1,\dots,t_\nu)$ are exactly the solutions of a system of homogeneous equations. |
| 707 | 600_2 | 90 | 85 | problem | unclear | Vorrichtung um einen Strom an mehreren Stellen beliebig einschalten und unterbrechen zu können u. zwar unabhängig von allen anderen Stellen | A device for being able to switch a current on and interrupt it at will at several places, and indeed independently of all other places | The page completes an induction step (from n to n-1) in the proof of the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality, sketches a circuit for switching a current on and off independently from two or more places, and poses sphere-packing and circle-packing questions together with a related polygon minimization problem. |
| 708 | 600_2 | 92 | 87 | problem | unclear | Aufgabe (Hurwitz): Unter allen Polygonen von $n$ Seiten und gegebenem Inhalt $J$ soll [-d-] die Summe der $\mu$ ten Potenze der Seiten ein Minimum [-?-] sein. | Problem (Hurwitz): Among all polygons with n sides and given area J, the sum of the μ-th powers of the sides should be a minimum. | Short notes: a remark on curve closure, whether polyhedra can be bent, a Hurwitz extremal problem on polygons, a plan to treat the rotation of a fluid-filled body with Klein's parameters, a question on force fields with only closed orbits (Darboux–Halphen), and the start of a statement on expanding continuous functions in series of rational integral functions. |
| 709 | 600_2 | 96 | 91 | problem | unclear | Fertig nicht definirt. Dieser Uebelstand wird schlimmer, je mehr man darüber nachdenkt | 'Finished' is not defined. This defect becomes worse the more one thinks about it. | Reflecting on Cantor's letters, Hilbert considers the problem of which collections may be regarded as 'finished' (complete) in set theory, and proposes formulating set-theoretic propositions without the word 'set' and treating terms such as 'irrational number' merely as names for things with specified properties. |
| 710 | 600_3 | 6 | 3 | problem | unclear | Man hat also nach Hülfssatz 3 2 ganze zu 2 prime Zahlen $\pi, \kappa$ in $k$ so zu bestimmen, dass für alle Primideale $\mathfrak{w}$ in $k$ gleichzeitig | So, by Lemma 3, one has to determine 2 integers π, κ in k, prime to 2, such that for all prime ideals w in k simultaneously [conditions (1)] hold and moreover π, κ have prescribed signs in the real fields conjugate to k. | Hilbert reduces the solvability of a ternary quadratic (norm) equation over a number field k to finding two integers π, κ satisfying conditions on Hilbert norm residue symbols at all prime ideals, using auxiliary lemmas (Hilfssätze) 1–3 and an auxiliary prime l. |
| 711 | 600_3 | 21 | [18] | problem | unclear | Die Funktion $\zeta(s, x) = \sum \frac{x^n}{n^s}$ $(n = [-0-]1, 2, \dots)$ [-ist-] ge- nügt keiner bekannten Differentialgl.. Beweise | The function zeta(s, x) = sum x^n / n^s (n = 1, 2, ...) satisfies no known differential equation. Proofs | The page discusses how hard it is to prove that a differential equation cannot be solved by a single quadrature, then asks how solids, liquids, gases, aether and electricity should be described under molecular theory using mean values and expansions in small parameters, and finally states that the function zeta(s,x) = sum x^n/n^s satisfies no known differential equation. |
| 712 | 600_3 | 37 | [34] | problem | unclear | Beweise (Franz Meyer), dass es eine Function $f(n)$ giebt, so dass jede der $\infty$ viele Limesgleichungen | Prove (Franz Meyer) that there is a function f(n) such that each of the infinitely many limit equations [...] is satisfied. | Hilbert plans to extend the parameter-expansion method from linear to higher-degree (nonlinear) differential equations and expects algebraic singularities including branch points, with examples y''=λy² and Δu=λu². He then begins constructing a function f(n) satisfying an infinite chain of iterated-logarithm limit equations, attributed to Franz Meyer. |
| 713 | 600_3 | 43 | [40] | problem | unclear | In der Lobatschefskyschen Geometrie eine Gerade construiren, die zu 2 gegebenen parallel ist | In Lobachevskian geometry, to construct a line that is parallel to 2 given lines. | The page argues that a Dedekind cut need not exist in a non-Archimedean number system, states that his geometries lead to non-Archimedean groups, and attempts a construction of a line parallel to two given lines in Lobachevskian geometry; that last section is crossed out. |
| 714 | 600_3 | 44 | 41 | problem | unclear | Die kürzeste Entfernung 2er nicht sich schneidender {+und nicht+} [-?-] parallelen Graden finden d.h. gemeinsame Senkrechte | Find the shortest distance between two lines that neither intersect nor are parallel, i.e. their common perpendicular. | A crossed-out geometric sketch about lines that neither intersect nor are parallel, using a chain of equal angles and segments, followed by a note on functional equations relating Dirichlet's principle, spherical functions, and electric charge distributions on spheres or circles. |

| 885 | 600_1 | 39 | [30] | claim | unclear | Letzteres kann aber nur geschehen, wenn, wie man durch Rechng zeigen kann die Fläche die Curve berührt oder durch eine Singu- larität derselben geht oder eine Doppellinie der Fläche durch die Curve geht. | But the latter can only happen if, as one can show by calculation, the surface touches the curve, or passes through a singularity of it, or a double line of the surface passes through the curve. | An argument by continuous variation of coefficients that if no plane meets an intersection curve in the full number of distinct points then no surface of order μ does either, followed by a sketch of how to prove that surfaces through the common points of 4 surfaces are, from some order on, linear combinations of them. |

| 930 | 600_1 | 84 | 75 | claim | unclear | Die aufeinanderfolgenden Krümmungskreise einer Kurve schneiden sich nicht, weil die Entfernung ihrer Mittelpunkte (die Sehne der Evolute) kleiner ist als die Differenz ihrer Radien (der Bogen der Evolute) | Successive osculating circles of a curve do not intersect, because the distance between their centres (the chord of the evolute) is smaller than the difference of their radii (the arc of the evolute). | Notes on a hypothetical prime-number machine and the limits of what it can prove, a struck-out remark on successive osculating circles of a curve, deciding primality in finite time, and a remark praising Weierstrass as the Euclid of analysis. |

| 948 | 600_1 | 93 | [84] | claim | unclear | Wenn $f_1 \ldots f_n$ eine endliche Anzahl von ein an- der verschiedener Nullstellen haben, so ist jede Form $\varphi$, welche f. dieselben Punkte verschwindet, eine ganze algebra- ische Funktion von $f_1 \ldots f_n$, wie man ebenso zeigt | If f_1,...,f_n have a finite number of mutually distinct zeros, then every form phi that vanishes at the same points is an integral algebraic function of f_1,...,f_n, as one shows in the same way. | The page records a task to prove that the inverse of a continuous nowhere-differentiable function is infinitely many-valued, a plan for a non-Gaussian probability theory modelled on non-Euclidean geometry, and a sketch showing that when the resultant of n forms is nonzero the variables are integral algebraic functions of the forms. |

| 950 | 600_1 | 95 | [86] | claim | unclear | Es folgt so auch leicht dass ein eindimensionales, algebraisches Gebilde mit continuirlichen Transformationen [-mit-] {+in+} sich, nothwendig rational sein muss. | It also follows easily in this way that a one-dimensional algebraic configuration with continuous transformations into itself must necessarily be rational. | Hilbert generalises Maurer's theorem to linear substitutions of an arbitrary algebraic configuration into itself in any number of dimensions, sketches a proof, and notes that it implies, and conversely follows from, the rationality of one-dimensional algebraic configurations admitting continuous transformations into themselves. |

| 980 | 600_1 | 117 | [108] | claim | unclear | Wenn eine Funktion von n Veränderlichen unendlich viele lineare Transformationen mit ganzzahli- gen Coefficienten in sich zulässt, so giebt es wie man mit dem Minkowskischen Satz beweisen kann, mindestens eine Substitution von unendlich hoher Ord- nung, welche die Funktion ungeändert lässt | If a function of n variables admits infinitely many linear transformations with integer coefficients into itself, then, as one can prove with Minkowski's theorem, there is at least one substitution of infinitely high order which leaves the function unchanged. | The page sketches a proof, using Minkowski's theorem, that a function of n variables admitting infinitely many integral linear transformations into itself is an ordinary or simultaneous semi-invariant, and then begins listing properties of invariants. |

| 987 | 600_1 | 126 | 117 | claim | unclear | Man sieht daher, um einer Invariantentheo für diese Transformationen aufzustellen wiederum vollständig mit dem Operations= symbol $\mathfrak{R} = \frac{\partial^2}{\partial\beta_{12}\partial\beta_{12}} - \frac{\partial^2}{\partial\beta_{11}\beta_{12}}$ aus. | One therefore sees that, in order to set up an invariant theory for these transformations, one again manages completely with the operation symbol R = ∂²/∂β12∂β12 − ∂²/∂β11 β12. | Linear substitutions of the variables u, w and the cogredient/contragredient variables ξ, x are composed with a further substitution, and it is noted that an invariant theory for these transformations again comes out entirely with a second-order differential operation symbol in the coefficients β. |

| 999 | 600_1 | 133 | [124] | claim | unclear | In diesem construire man das Gebilde $U = 0$ welches den Raum nicht theilt, und welche sich als Verdichtungsgebilde der equivalenten Bereiche ergiebt. | In this [space] one should construct the locus U = 0, which does not divide the space and which turns out to be the accumulation locus of the equivalent domains. | Hilbert considers a function f of the coefficients u_ik of a quadratic form ψ, defined as an infinite sum of 1/ψ^s over all integer vectors, and plans to study its invariance substitutions, a fundamental domain in complex u-space, and the fixed points of the substitutions, beginning with the case n = 3. |

| 1003 | 600_1 | 142 | 133 | claim | unclear | da im Kreistheilungskörper bekannt- lich die Idealtheorie auf die Theorie der höheren Congruenz sich allein stützen lässt | since in the cyclotomic field, as is well known, ideal theory can be based solely on the theory of higher congruences | A list of research problems and plans: shortening Kummer's proof in cyclotomic fields, extending the Pascal hexagon's notable points, Euler's Diophantine problem, division of the lemniscate arc into a+ib parts, transferring Staude's string construction of the ellipsoid, and treating the fields √(a+bi) and ∛α to derive reciprocity laws. |
| 1004 | 600_2 | 4 | 0 | claim | unclear | Einen idealen Warscheinlichkeitsapparat (Glücksrad) construiren, hiesse eine Naturer= scheinung ausfindig machen, in welcher kein Naturgesetzt herrscht, und das läuft unserem Denken zuwider. | To construct an ideal probability apparatus (wheel of fortune) would mean finding a natural phenomenon in which no natural law holds, and that runs contrary to our thinking. | Three short notes: proving that the Bernoulli function (polynomial) is irreducible, using its behaviour modulo a prime degree p; a remark that an ideal probability device (wheel of fortune) is impossible; and a task to set up a theory of calculation. |
| 1005 | 600_2 | 6 | 1 | claim | unclear | Dieser Lindemannsche Ansatz führt aber nur in dem ganz besondern Falle, dass $\varphi(z,z')=z^n+z'^n+1$ [?] ist. | This approach of Lindemann's, however, only leads [to the goal] in the very special case that $\varphi(z,z')=z^n+z'^n+1$. | Prompted by a lecture of Lindemann, Hilbert considers whether every function mapping the interior of an oval of an algebraic curve onto a half-plane satisfies an ordinary algebraic differential equation, and describes Lindemann's approach and its limitation. |
| 1006 | 600_2 | 7 | [2] | claim | unclear | Bemerkt sei, dass die Brennpunkte der algebraischen Curve $f(x,y)=0$ den Verzwei- gungspunkten entsprechen, [-weil-] da dieselben durch $\frac{\partial f}{\partial z}=0$ bestimmt sind. | Let it be remarked that the foci of the algebraic curve f(x,y)=0 correspond to the branch points, since these are determined by ∂f/∂z=0. | The page finishes a remark that the branch points of an algebraic curve f(x,y)=0 correspond to points where ∂f/∂z=0, then sets the task of treating the different representations of a ternary definite form as a quotient of sums of squares by continuously varying the coefficients, with the case where φ=ψ=χ=0 share a common point being the critical one. |

| 1012 | 600_2 | 12 | 7 | claim | unclear | Das aber[?] bezeichnete Lindemannsche Problem kann auf folgen- de Aufgabe zurückgeführt werden | The Lindemann problem referred to above can be reduced to the following task. | Notes posing several problems: expanding e^π = i^i into a simple continued fraction, a reduction of a problem of Lindemann's to finding rational functions invariant under a substitution, the meaning of Jacobi's inversion problem and Abel's theorem for genus p = ∞, and the behaviour of the second root when a root is approximated by a quadratic equation. |
| 1013 | 600_2 | 12 | 7 | claim | unclear | Übrigens ist die Lindemannsche Ueber- legung garnicht nöthig, da man, um eine auf der X-Axe reelle Funktion zu erhalten, ja nur $x = \frac{z + z'}{2}$ als [-A-] analytische Funktion von Z zu betrachten braucht. | Incidentally, Lindemann's argument is not necessary at all, since to obtain a function that is real on the X-axis one need only regard x = (z + z')/2 as an analytic function of Z. | Notes posing several problems: expanding e^π = i^i into a simple continued fraction, a reduction of a problem of Lindemann's to finding rational functions invariant under a substitution, the meaning of Jacobi's inversion problem and Abel's theorem for genus p = ∞, and the behaviour of the second root when a root is approximated by a quadratic equation. |
| 1014 | 600_2 | 13 | [8] | claim | unclear | Die von Saalschütz durch Rechnung be- wiesene Thatsache, dass die Entwicklung $e^{-\frac{1}{1-x}} = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \dots$ auch auf dem Einheitskreise überall convergirt, folgt aus Fourriers Entwicklung | The fact, proved by Saalschütz through computation, that the expansion $e^{-1/(1-x)} = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \dots$ converges everywhere on the unit circle as well, follows from Fourier's expansion. | Hilbert notes that the convergence of the power series of e^{-1/(1-x)} everywhere on the unit circle, shown by Saalschütz through computation, follows from Fourier expansion, and he begins stating a general theorem on convergence on the boundary of the circle of convergence when there are finitely many singular points. |
| 1015 | 600_2 | 13 | [8] | claim | unclear | Ueberhaupt hat man den allgemeinen Satz: Liegen auf der Peripherie des Convergenz-Kreises nur eine endliche Anzahl von singulären Stellen, und ist jede von der Beschaffenheit, dass beim Hi- neingehn, längs der Peripherie in dieselbe die Function von niedrer Ordnung als der | In general one has the general theorem: If only a finite number of singular points lie on the periphery of the circle of convergence, and each is of such a nature that, on approaching it along the periphery, the function [becomes infinite] of lower order than the... | Hilbert notes that the convergence of the power series of e^{-1/(1-x)} everywhere on the unit circle, shown by Saalschütz through computation, follows from Fourier expansion, and he begins stating a general theorem on convergence on the boundary of the circle of convergence when there are finitely many singular points. |
| 1016 | 600_2 | 14 | 9 | claim | unclear | so convergirt die Potenzreihe auf jeder nicht singulären Stelle der Periphe- rie | then the power series converges at every non-singular point of the circumference | The page concludes a remark on convergence of a power series on the boundary circle, then begins treating a curve f(x,y)=0 through the origin at an ordinary point, expanding x as a power series in y and dividing f(φ(y)+r, y) by r to obtain F(x,y). |
| 1017 | 600_2 | 15 | [10] | claim | unclear | Wenn $k-2$ Wendepunkte in den Nullpunkt hinein fallen, so ist die Entwicklung von der Gestalt $x = \alpha_k y^k + \ldots$ vorausgesetzt, dass der Null- punkt kein singulärer Punkt ist. | If k-2 inflection points fall into the origin, then the expansion is of the form x = α_k y^k + ..., provided that the origin is not a singular point. | Computing the lowest power of y in the Hessian of a curve f after substituting x = φ(y), and concluding that exactly k-2 inflection points fall into the origin when the branch has expansion x = α_k y^k + ..., provided the origin is not singular. |
| 1018 | 600_2 | 16 | 11 | claim | unclear | dass eine Curve $n$ter Ordnung höchstens $n-2$fache Wendepunkte haben darf, und folglich min- destens $3n$ getrennt liegende Wendepunkte ha- ben muss. | that a curve of order n may have at most (n-2)-fold inflection points, and consequently must have at least 3n separately lying inflection points. | The page covers the maximum multiplicity of inflection points of a plane curve of order n, with the example x^n+y^n+1=0. | 1020 | 600_2 | 18 | 13 | claim | unclear | Dieser Nachweis ge- lingt wenn man die zu suchende Function durch rationale Functionen gleichmäsig an- nähert und dann durch partielle Integra- tion erkennt, dass diese rationalen Functionen einen gewissen Differentialausdruck zum ver- | This proof succeeds if one uniformly approximates the function to be found by rational functions and then recognizes by partial integration that these rational functions [make] a certain differential expression [vanish]... | Hilbert finishes a proof that an approximating function must be divisible by x^2 and proposes generalizing the result to the calculus of variations, to show that the extremizing function possesses higher derivatives via uniform approximation by rational functions and partial integration. |

| 1022 | 600_2 | 20 | 15 | claim | unclear | Dabei wird es nöthig sein den Existenzbeweis mittelst des Cauchyschen Integrals in geeigneter Weise Zahlentheoretisch umzumodeln und hinein zu arbeiten. | For this it will be necessary to remodel the existence proof via the Cauchy integral in a suitable number-theoretic way and to work it in. | Research plans: studying prime ideals following Kronecker via the field y^2+1=0, re-proving Gauss's representation of primes as sums of two squares via the function field x^2+y^2=p, proving Dirichlet's unit theorem without the existence theorem for roots, and counting primes for which 2 is a primitive root. |

| 1032 | 600_2 | 24 | 19 | claim | unclear | Jede Zahl $\omega$ des Oberkörpers kann mit einer geeigneten Zahl $c$ welche zu einer beliebig gegebenen Zahl $\alpha$ prim ist, multipli= cirt, in die Gestalt gebracht werden $\omega=\tau_1\varepsilon_1+\dots$ $+$ [-$\tau_\mu\varepsilon$-] $\tau_\mu\varepsilon_\mu$, wo $\varepsilon_1,\dots,\varepsilon_\mu$ gewisse feste $\mu$ ganze Zahlen des Oberkörpers und $\tau_1,\dots,\tau_\mu$ ganze Zah= len | Every number ω of the extension field, multiplied by a suitable number c that is prime to an arbitrarily given number α, can be brought into the form ω = τ_1ε_1 + ... + τ_με_μ, where ε_1,...,ε_μ are certain fixed μ integers of the extension field and τ_1,...,τ_μ are integers of the base field. | Toward showing that the discriminant of the base field divides the discriminant of the extension field of degree n = νμ, Hilbert begins proving a lemma: every integer ω of the extension, multiplied by a suitable number prime to a given α, can be written as a combination τ_1ε_1+...+τ_με_μ with fixed integers ε_i of the extension and integers τ_i of the base field. |
| 1033 | 600_2 | 25 | [20] | claim | unclear | $c^{\frac{\mu \mu-1}{2}} D_0^{\frac{1}{2}}$, wo $D_0$ die Diskriminante der Oberkörpers ist, wird gleich der De= terminante | c^{μ(μ-1)/2} D_0^{1/2}, where D_0 is the discriminant of the upper field, becomes equal to the determinant. | Hilbert completes a proof that, after multiplication by a suitable number c_1, every integer of a relative field can be written as a combination of powers with coefficients from the base field, proves the auxiliary lemma used, and starts relating the discriminant of the upper field to a determinant. |
| 1034 | 600_2 | 25 | [20] | claim | unclear | Offenbar ist dann jede ganze Zahl des Oberkör= pers $\omega$ {+mit $c_1$ multiplicirt+} in die Gestalt $\omega = t_1 + t_2 \varepsilon + \dots + \tau_\mu \varepsilon_\mu$ bringbar | Obviously then every integer ω of the upper field, multiplied by c_1, can be brought into the form ω = t_1 + t_2 ε + ... + τ_μ ε_μ. | Hilbert completes a proof that, after multiplication by a suitable number c_1, every integer of a relative field can be written as a combination of powers with coefficients from the base field, proves the auxiliary lemma used, and starts relating the discriminant of the upper field to a determinant. |
| 1035 | 600_2 | 27 | [22] | claim | unclear | Der Satz: Wenn eine $\infty$e Reihe eine stetige Function darstellt, so convergirt die Reihe gleichmässig, ist unrichtig. | The theorem 'If an infinite series represents a continuous function, then the series converges uniformly' is incorrect. | Short notes on Gauss's problem about how often irregular and regular negative determinants occur, a claim that a continuous series-sum need not converge uniformly, a preference for Weierstrass's convergence method over Eisenstein–Kronecker's, and the start of a discussion of irrational numbers defined by fundamental sequences. |
| 1036 | 600_2 | 27 | [22] | claim | unclear | In der Theorie der elliptischen Functionen und der höheren Transcendenten ist die Weierstrass'sche Metho- de des Convergentmachens einer unendlichen Reihe der Eisenstein Kronecker'schen vorzuziehen | In the theory of elliptic functions and higher transcendentals, Weierstrass's method of making an infinite series convergent is preferable to that of Eisenstein and Kronecker. | Short notes on Gauss's problem about how often irregular and regular negative determinants occur, a claim that a continuous series-sum need not converge uniformly, a preference for Weierstrass's convergence method over Eisenstein–Kronecker's, and the start of a discussion of irrational numbers defined by fundamental sequences. |
| 1037 | 600_2 | 30 | 25 | claim | unclear | Der eben genannte Satz ergiebt [-s-] sich im Allgemeinen als unrichtig [-...-] nämlich nur dann, wenn $p$ nicht in der Discriminante aufgeht; | The theorem just mentioned turns out to be incorrect in general; namely, it holds only when p does not divide the discriminant. | Hilbert discusses when a polynomial f(x) splits modulo powers of a prime p in the same way as modulo p, notes that this fails when p divides the discriminant, and begins a proof using a Galois field, a prime ideal of p and the subfield attached to the group of substitutions fixing that prime ideal. |
| 1038 | 600_2 | 30 | 25 | claim | unclear | Nun beweisen wir den Satz: Wenn [-...-] eine Zahl $\alpha \equiv$ einer Zahl in $V$ wird nach jeder Potenz von $\mathfrak{p}$, so ist sie selbst eine Zahl in $V$. | Now we prove the theorem: if a number α becomes congruent to a number in V modulo every power of 𝔭, then it is itself a number in V. | Hilbert discusses when a polynomial f(x) splits modulo powers of a prime p in the same way as modulo p, notes that this fails when p divides the discriminant, and begins a proof using a Galois field, a prime ideal of p and the subfield attached to the group of substitutions fixing that prime ideal. |

| 1042 | 600_2 | 36 | 31 | claim | unclear | dadurch erhält mann dann den Rechnungsgesetzen $a b = b a$, $a+b = b+a$, $a(b+c) = ab+ac$ entspre- chend den [-Pa-] Pascalschen Satz für ein Geradenpaar und 2 andere Schliessungssätze. | In this way one then obtains the arithmetic laws ab = ba, a+b = b+a, a(b+c) = ab+ac, corresponding to Pascal's theorem for a pair of lines and 2 other closure theorems. | Geometric constructions of segment multiplication and addition, showing that the laws ab = ba, a+b = b+a and a(b+c) = ab+ac correspond to Pascal's theorem for a pair of lines and two other closure theorems, and that a collinearity condition must be added. |

| 1048 | 600_2 | 41 | [36] | claim | unclear | Die überall stattfindende Convexität eines Körpers wie sie bei Minkowski auftritt und von mir in der Arbeit die gerade Linie als die kürzeste verwandt wird ist gewissermassen die Invariante des Begriffes zwischen. | The everywhere-holding convexity of a body, as it appears in Minkowski and as used by me in the paper on the straight line as the shortest, is in a certain sense the invariant of the concept 'between'. | The page completes a proof that the coefficients G of an equation satisfied by the product ab are integral (homogeneous) functions of the c, citing Acta vol. 6 p. 71, and then remarks that the convexity of a body in Minkowski's work and the straight line as shortest path in Hilbert's own work are in a sense the invariant of the concept 'between'. |

| 1061 | 600_2 | 53 | [48] | claim | unclear | so ist damit, indem man alle Schritte entsprechend mit diesem $\underset{\delta=0}{L}$ ausführt die Möglichkeit gegeben den Nachweis der Transcen- denz von $e$ ohne Integralrechnung zu führen. | Thus, by carrying out all steps correspondingly with this limit as delta goes to 0, one has the possibility of giving the proof of the transcendence of e without integral calculus. | Two exercises: proving that a polynomial with binomial coefficients raised to a power alpha has all real roots for alpha > 1 and all imaginary roots for alpha < 1, and a plan to prove the irrationality/transcendence of e and pi without integrals by replacing the integral with the limit of a sum. |
| 1062 | 600_2 | 54 | 49 | claim | unclear | Ebenso ist auch in der Mathematik die Ausdehnung und Viel- verzweigtheit einer Theorie, wenn dieselbe zum Beweise eines einfachen Satzes, nöthig ist, an sich kein Fehler. | Likewise in mathematics, the extent and many-branchedness of a theory, if it is necessary for the proof of a simple theorem, is not in itself a fault. | A remark that an extensive theory is not a fault even if it is needed to prove a simple theorem, with an analogy to a letter and its reply, followed by notes on finding infinitely many rational points on cubic curves by tangent construction and on extending this to x^4+y^4=1, the quintic surface x^5+y^5+z^5=1, and the diophantine problems of Euler and Legendre. |
| 1063 | 600_2 | 54 | 49 | claim | unclear | Wenn von einer cubischen Curve mit rationalen Zahlcoefficienten eine rational ist, so kann man durch Tangentenconstruction $\infty$ viele finden; | If one [point] of a cubic curve with rational number coefficients is rational, then one can find infinitely many by tangent construction; | A remark that an extensive theory is not a fault even if it is needed to prove a simple theorem, with an analogy to a letter and its reply, followed by notes on finding infinitely many rational points on cubic curves by tangent construction and on extending this to x^4+y^4=1, the quintic surface x^5+y^5+z^5=1, and the diophantine problems of Euler and Legendre. |
| 1064 | 600_2 | 55 | [50] | claim | unclear | Also es giebt jedenfalls Zahlen, die die Darstellung beliebig oft gestatten. | So there are in any case numbers that admit the representation arbitrarily often. | The page gives literature references on orthogonal substitution coefficients and on functions with periodic continued fraction expansions, a remark on Cantor's table of representations of even numbers as sums of two primes with an averaging estimate, and the idea of mappings between n-dimensional regions governed by linear PDEs as a generalisation of conformal mapping. |
| 1065 | 600_2 | 56 | 51 | claim | unclear | ein solcher Schnelldampfer ist die Idealtheorie. | Such a fast steamer is the theory of ideals. | The page ends a note on functions of solutions of linear differential equations, proposing the case n=3 and physics equations as leads, then gives an analogy that ideal theory is like a fast steamer reaching a goal more quickly than a long chain of elementary procedures. |
| 1066 | 600_2 | 57 | [52] | claim | unclear | Hat nun keine dieser Potenzreihen lauter reelle Coefficienten dann tritt Maximum oder Minimum ein, sonst nicht | If none of these power series has all real coefficients, then a maximum or minimum occurs; otherwise not. | A note on Weierstrass's criterion for deciding whether a function of two variables f(x,y) has a maximum or minimum at the origin, by solving f(x,y)=0 for y as power series in fractional powers of x and checking whether any has all real coefficients. |
| 1067 | 600_2 | 57 | [52] | claim | unclear | Nach Weierstrass ist zur Entscheidung darüber ob eine Function von mehr Veränderlichen $f(x\,y)$ ein Maximum oder Minimum hat - an der Stelle $x=0$, $y=0$ nur nöthig aus der Gleichung $f(x,y)=0$ y als Potenzreihe von $x^{\frac{1}{n}}$ darzustellen. | According to Weierstrass, in order to decide whether a function of several variables f(x,y) has a maximum or minimum at the point x=0, y=0, it is only necessary to represent y from the equation f(x,y)=0 as a power series in x^{1/n}. | A note on Weierstrass's criterion for deciding whether a function of two variables f(x,y) has a maximum or minimum at the origin, by solving f(x,y)=0 for y as power series in fractional powers of x and checking whether any has all real coefficients. |
| 1068 | 600_2 | 57 | [52] | claim | unclear | so würde in diesem Sinne ein Maximum oder Mini- mum auch dann noch eintreten können, wenn eine Potenzreihe mit lauter reellen Coefficienten existirt, es muss dann aber der erste partielle Differentialquo- tient von $f(x,y)$ nach $y$, welcher nach Einsetzung dieser Potenzreihe nicht identisch in $x$ verschwindet von ungerader Ordnung sein. | In this sense a maximum or minimum could still occur even if a power series with all real coefficients exists; but then the first partial derivative of f(x,y) with respect to y that does not vanish identically in x after substituting this power series must be of odd order. | A note on Weierstrass's criterion for deciding whether a function of two variables f(x,y) has a maximum or minimum at the origin, by solving f(x,y)=0 for y as power series in fractional powers of x and checking whether any has all real coefficients. |

(continues on next page) | The page discusses discriminants of algebraic fields (Körper) and of an algebraic 'Bund', the claimed theorem D(y,x)=D(x,y), a note doubting these theorems, and the start of a generalisation of Picard's theorems. |
| 1070 | 600_2 | 60 | 55 | claim | unclear | Die elliptischen Functionen sind nichts andres als die Nicht-Euklidische Geometrie auf einem Rin- ge vom Geschlechte $p=1$. | Elliptic functions are nothing other than non-Euclidean geometry on a ring of genus p=1. | The page bounds the genus of algebraic relations between transcendental entire functions, sketches elliptic functions as non-Euclidean geometry on a genus-1 ring with distance as the elliptic integral of the third kind, and asks whether 11 consecutive ones occur in the decimal expansion of pi. |
