| 1072 | 600_2 | 61 | [56] | claim | unclear | wenn dann $A$ in $K$ nach jeder Potenz von $\mathfrak{p}$ $\equiv$ ist einer Zahl [-$\alpha$-] in $k$, so ist $A$ eine Zahl in $k$. | If A in K is congruent, modulo every power of 𝔭, to a number in k, then A is a number in k. | Notes on a sum of products of Epstein-type zeta functions Z(s,ω)Z(1−s,ω) and its relation to Kronecker's limit formula, followed by arguments that the common subfield of two Galois fields is Galois and that a number fixed by a substitution group modulo all powers of a prime ideal lies in the fixed field. |
| 1073 | 600_2 | 62 | 57 | claim | unclear | Zusammenhang mit der Darstellung einer quadratischen Form als Summe von Quadra- ten. | Connection with the representation of a quadratic form as a sum of squares. | Notes on the problem of approximation or interpolation, distinguishing the character of the approximating functions from the principle of approximation (agreement of power-series terms or least squares), and linking least squares to writing a quadratic form as a sum of squares. |
| 1074 | 600_2 | 63 | [58] | claim | unclear | Mit Hilfe der Kugelfunctionen beweist man leicht den Satz: Wenn $f(x)$ eine rationale Func- tion $m$ ten Grades ist und $\int_{-1}^{+1} f^2(x)\,dx = 1$ | With the help of spherical functions one easily proves the theorem: If f(x) is a rational function of m-th degree and the integral from -1 to +1 of f^2(x) dx = 1 | The page discusses least-squares approximation over [-1,1] by polynomials plus sqrt(x^2-1) times polynomials, generalisations to other function systems and side conditions, literature on continued fractions, and begins a theorem about polynomials normalised by an integral, proved via spherical (Legendre) functions. |
| 1075 | 600_2 | 64 | 59 | claim | unclear | so bleibt das Maximum [-der-] der Function $f(x)$ im Intervall $-1$ bis $+1$, unter einer von $n$ ab- hängigen Grenze. | then the maximum of the function f(x) in the interval -1 to +1 remains below a bound depending on n. | The page ends a bound on the maximum of a function on the interval -1 to +1, compares the union of arithmetic, function theory and geometry in the theory of multi-variable analogues of elliptic functions to the ingredients of gunpowder, and begins a generalization of Cauchy's theorem to two complex variables on an algebraic surface. |
| 1076 | 600_2 | 65 | [60] | claim | unclear | Auch ist diese ganze Zahl $m$ davon unabhängig welche der 3 Veränderlichen man als die unab- hängige betrachtet. | Also this integer m is independent of which of the 3 variables one regards as the independent one. | The page defines how often a pair of algebraic functions φ and ψ on an algebraic curve f=0 jointly take a value pair (a,b) at a point, notes that this multiplicity is independent of the choice of independent variable, states that every value pair is taken equally often in total, and notes connections to ideal theory. |
| 1077 | 600_2 | 65 | [60] | claim | unclear | Das Func- tionenpaar $\varphi$ und $\psi$ nimt auf $f=0$ jedes Wer- thepaar $a$, $b$ im Ganzen gleich oft an, wird also insbesondere gleich oft $0$ und gleich oft unendlich. | The function pair φ and ψ takes every value pair a, b on f=0 equally often in total, and hence in particular becomes 0 equally often and infinite equally often. | The page defines how often a pair of algebraic functions φ and ψ on an algebraic curve f=0 jointly take a value pair (a,b) at a point, notes that this multiplicity is independent of the choice of independent variable, states that every value pair is taken equally often in total, and notes connections to ideal theory. |
| 1078 | 600_2 | 65 | [60] | claim | unclear | Diese selbe ganze Zahl $m$ gibt auch zugleich an wie oft die Function $\varphi$ als Function von $x$ be- trachtet, auf $f=0$, $\psi-b=0$ den Werth $a$ annimt. | This same integer m also indicates at the same time how often the function φ, regarded as a function of x, takes the value a on f=0, ψ−b=0. | The page defines how often a pair of algebraic functions φ and ψ on an algebraic curve f=0 jointly take a value pair (a,b) at a point, notes that this multiplicity is independent of the choice of independent variable, states that every value pair is taken equally often in total, and notes connections to ideal theory. |
| 1079 | 600_2 | 66 | 61 | claim | unclear | Die analy- tische Function heisst regulär in $z$ und $z'$ und ist in eine Potenzreihe entwickelbar, wie man durch die Cauchy'sche Integral- darstellung leicht zeigt. | The analytic function is called regular in z and z' and can be expanded in a power series, as one easily shows by means of the Cauchy integral representation. | The page asks in what sense the arithmetic-geometric mean method exemplifies defining a transcendental function as a limit of algebraic functions, then states the conditions under which a pair of real functions of four real variables defines a regular analytic function of two complex variables, and begins a statement about single-valued functions rational in y. |
| 1080 | 600_2 | 68 | 63 | claim | unclear | Die Gruppe aller linearen ganzzahligen Substitutionen der n Variablen $x_1, \dots x_n$ ist eine {+un+}eigentliche, dagegen die daraus hervorgehende [-holoed-] isomorphe Gruppe linearer ganzzahliger Substitutionen der $n^2$ Variablen $a_{11}, \dots, a_{nn}$ ist eine eigentliche | The group of all linear integral substitutions of the n variables x_1,...,x_n is an improper one, whereas the isomorphic group of linear integral substitutions of the n^2 variables a_11,...,a_nn arising from it is a proper one. | On the reduced space (fundamental domain) in the n^2-dimensional space of coefficients of n linear forms, which is bounded by finitely many hyperplanes and nowhere concave; a question (attributed to Minkowski) on whether its volume for A=1 is finite, and a sketch of a proof principle using a growing cube and its equivalent convex bodies, together with remarks on the group of unimodular integral su |
| 1081 | 600_2 | 68 | 63 | claim | unclear | Die unendlich vielen Räume [-stossen an-] verdich- ten sich nur da wo die [-Determinante-] {+A+} obiger n Linearformen verschwindet. | The infinitely many spaces accumulate only where A of the above n linear forms vanishes. | On the reduced space (fundamental domain) in the n^2-dimensional space of coefficients of n linear forms, which is bounded by finitely many hyperplanes and nowhere concave; a question (attributed to Minkowski) on whether its volume for A=1 is finite, and a sketch of a proof principle using a growing cube and its equivalent convex bodies, together with remarks on the group of unimodular integral su |
| 1082 | 600_2 | 68 | 63 | claim | unclear | Es gilt der Satz, dass der reducirte Raum im Raume von $n^2$ Dimensionen durch eine endliche Anzahl von Ebenen begrenzt und zwar ein nirgends conkaver Raum ist. | The theorem holds that the reduced space in the space of n^2 dimensions is bounded by a finite number of planes and is moreover a nowhere-concave space. | On the reduced space (fundamental domain) in the n^2-dimensional space of coefficients of n linear forms, which is bounded by finitely many hyperplanes and nowhere concave; a question (attributed to Minkowski) on whether its volume for A=1 is finite, and a sketch of a proof principle using a growing cube and its equivalent convex bodies, together with remarks on the group of unimodular integral su |
| 1083 | 600_2 | 69 | [64] | claim | unclear | Es giebt nun stets einen 2ten Be- | There is now always a second point of contact, which arises from the first by a certain transformation and against which a third nowhere-concave body borders. | Hilbert sketches building a fundamental domain (Urbereich) for a substitution group by expanding convex bodies until they touch and fill space, proposes using it for automorphic functions linked to higher continued-fraction algorithms, and begins the case n=2 with lattice conditions for a reduced region. |
| 1084 | 600_2 | 69 | [64] | claim | unclear | und hinreichenden Bedingungen: $\xi_1 \geqq \xi_2, \eta_2 \geqq \xi_1 \geqq \eta_1, \xi_1, \xi_2, \eta_1, \eta_2 \geqq 0$ | One obtains the following necessary and sufficient conditions for the reduced region: xi_1 >= xi_2, eta_2 >= xi_1 >= eta_1, xi_1, xi_2, eta_1, eta_2 >= 0. | Hilbert sketches building a fundamental domain (Urbereich) for a substitution group by expanding convex bodies until they touch and fill space, proposes using it for automorphic functions linked to higher continued-fraction algorithms, and begins the case n=2 with lattice conditions for a reduced region. |
| 1085 | 600_2 | 70 | 65 | claim | unclear | Daher soll man auch pädagogisch die scharfen Hilfsmittel möglichst früh bringen, und sich durch keine Rücksicht auf Ueberlieferung von diesem Princip abbringen lassen. | Therefore, in teaching too, one should introduce the sharp tools as early as possible, and not let any regard for tradition dissuade one from this principle. | Reflections on oral tradition alongside learning from books (Hilbert learned Weierstrass's theory from Hurwitz), on why science is not overwhelmed by publications, on teaching sharper tools early, and the start of a remark on mathematical disciplines arising from physics. |
| 1086 | 600_2 | 70 | 65 | claim | unclear | Daraus kann man aber nicht die Nothwendigkeit herleiten, dass diese Wissen- | But from this one cannot derive the necessity that these sciences- (sentence continues on next page) | Reflections on oral tradition alongside learning from books (Hilbert learned Weierstrass's theory from Hurwitz), on why science is not overwhelmed by publications, on teaching sharper tools early, and the start of a remark on mathematical disciplines arising from physics. |
| 1087 | 600_2 | 70 | 65 | claim | unclear | Weil stets schärfere Hilfsmittel erstehn, welche das frühere und noch mehr leisten, und somit das frühere tödten. | Because ever sharper tools keep arising, which accomplish what the earlier ones did and still more, and thus kill off the earlier ones. | Reflections on oral tradition alongside learning from books (Hilbert learned Weierstrass's theory from Hurwitz), on why science is not overwhelmed by publications, on teaching sharper tools early, and the start of a remark on mathematical disciplines arising from physics. |
| 1088 | 600_2 | 71 | [66] | claim | unclear | Dass im Falle $l=2$ der Satz {+, dass, wenn $K$ in bezug auf $k$ relativ Abelsch vom Relativen Primzahlgrade $l$ und der Relativ- discriminante $1$ ist, in $k$ stets ein nicht $1$ zu-[?] der [?] Ideal existirt, welches in $K$ Hauptideal wird+} nicht stets zu gelten braucht, zeigt das Beispiel: | That in the case l=2 the theorem — that if K is relatively Abelian over k of relative prime degree l and relative discriminant 1, there always exists in k a non-[trivial] ideal which becomes a principal ideal in K — need not always hold is shown by the example: | The page ends a remark on mathematics as an autonomous, unified discipline, defines reduced quadratic and decomposable forms with a finiteness statement, and begins a counterexample for relative degree 2 to a statement about ideals of a base field becoming principal in an unramified relative Abelian extension. |
| 1089 | 600_2 | 73 | [68] | claim | unclear | Das zahlentheoretische Analogon zu meinem Satze über Irreducibilität einer ganzzahligen Gleichung mit Parameter in Crelle gilt nicht | The number-theoretic analogue of my theorem on the irreducibility of an integer equation with a parameter in Crelle does not hold | The page closes a remark on methods of mastering a whole through infinitely small neighbourhoods or congruences modulo a modulus, asks about a function obtained by substituting a quotient involving eta, t, e and omega into J(omega) and its possible connection with an Eisenstein-type zeta series, and begins a note on the number-theoretic analogue of Hilbert's irreducibility theorem. |
| 1090 | 600_2 | 73 | [68] | claim | unclear | so entsteht eine eindeutige Function von $t$ und $J(\omega)$ | then a single-valued function of t and J(omega) arises | The page closes a remark on methods of mastering a whole through infinitely small neighbourhoods or congruences modulo a modulus, asks about a function obtained by substituting a quotient involving eta, t, e and omega into J(omega) and its possible connection with an Eisenstein-type zeta series, and begins a note on the number-theoretic analogue of Hilbert's irreducibility theorem. |
| 1091 | 600_2 | 74 | 69 | claim | unclear | dann folgt die Weierstrass- sche Potenzreihenentwicklung durch die Cauchy'sche Integraldarstellung unmittelbar | then the Weierstrassian power series expansion follows immediately from the Cauchy integral representation | The page discusses irreducible integer equations that become reducible modulo every prime, gives the example built from the square roots of 2 and 3, and proposes an alternative definition of an analytic function of two complex variables. |
| 1092 | 600_2 | 74 | 69 | claim | unclear | nach meinem Satz in den Göttinger Nachrichten gilt dies von jeder Galois- schen nicht durch Wurzelziehen auflösbaren Gleichung | according to my theorem in the Göttinger Nachrichten, this holds for every Galois equation not solvable by radicals | The page discusses irreducible integer equations that become reducible modulo every prime, gives the example built from the square roots of 2 and 3, and proposes an alternative definition of an analytic function of two complex variables. |
| 1093 | 600_2 | 74 | 69 | claim | unclear | weil für jede [-Zahl-] Primzahl $p$ entweder 2 oder 3, oder $2\cdot 3=6$ quadra- tischer Rest | because for every prime p either 2 or 3 or 2·3=6 is a quadratic residue | The page discusses irreducible integer equations that become reducible modulo every prime, gives the example built from the square roots of 2 and 3, and proposes an alternative definition of an analytic function of two complex variables. |
| 1094 | 600_2 | 75 | [70] | claim | unclear | Die Fortsetzung der Potentiale, auch der räum- lichen ist dann und nur dann gestattet wenn die [-Randfläche-] Randcurve (Randfläche und die Randfunction analytisch ist. | The continuation of potentials, including spatial ones, is permitted if and only if the boundary curve (boundary surface) and the boundary function are analytic. | Short notes on constructing all non-solvable quintic equations with prescribed discriminant, on when continuation of potentials is permitted, and remarks on the critical period in mathematics and on general theories. |
| 1095 | 600_2 | 77 | [72] | claim | unclear | In jedem noch so kleinen Gebiete einer Rie- mann'schen Fläche liegen Punkte die als Prim- ideale aufgefasst ein Product ergeben, dass in einer vorgelegten Klasse liegt. | In every region of a Riemann surface, however small, there lie points which, regarded as prime ideals, yield a product that lies in a given class. | Three research notes: simultaneous rational approximation of (1-z)^α and (1-z)^{-α} as a route to proving 2^√2 irrational; obtaining all relatively Abelian fields over an algebraic function field by division of Abelian functions; and a claim, with the start of a proof, that every small region of a Riemann surface contains points whose product as prime ideals lies in a given class. |
| 1096 | 600_2 | 78 | 73 | claim | unclear | Wenn eine analytische Curve mit einem Stück auf einer analytischen Fläche liegt braucht sie nicht ganz auf ihr zu liegen. | If an analytic curve lies with one piece on an analytic surface, it need not lie entirely on it. | Notes on obtaining an algebraic function of a surface by truncating power series, a remark that an analytic curve partly on an analytic surface need not lie wholly on it (citing Weierstrass), and a characterization of Hadamard's functions by the growth bound $\|f\| < e^{\|z\|^m}$ with a coefficient estimate. |
| 1097 | 600_2 | 81 | [76] | claim | unclear | Die Ent= deckung solcher Functionen würde ein neues Band zwischen Zahlen und Functionen= theorie knüpfen zum Segen für beide Disciplinen, und für die gesammte mathe= matische Wissenschaft überhaupt | The discovery of such functions would tie a new bond between number theory and function theory, to the benefit of both disciplines and of mathematical science as a whole. | A remark that giving ever new proofs of quadratic reciprocity is pointless, followed by the suggestion to seek functions belonging to arbitrary number fields as the exponential function belongs to the rationals and elliptic modular functions to imaginary quadratic fields. |
| 1098 | 600_2 | 82 | 77 | claim | unclear | Einfachster Fall: die Gleichg $x_1 = \varphi_1(t_1, t_2)$, $x_2 = \varphi_2(t_1, t_2)$ sind in $t$ für[?] alle $x_1, x_2$ nach $t_1, t_2$ auflösbar, sobald zwischen[?] den homog. Fkt.[?] $\varphi_1, \varphi_2$ keine Identität besteht. | Simplest case: the equations $x_1=\varphi_1(t_1,t_2)$, $x_2=\varphi_2(t_1,t_2)$ are solvable for $t_1, t_2$ for all $x_1, x_2$, as soon as no identity holds between the homogeneous functions $\varphi_1, \varphi_2$. | The page ends a remark that function theory draws its strength from its border with number theory and should follow arithmetic, gives an aphorism about the heights of arithmetic, and then sets a problem with a proof sketch: show that points given by homogeneous parametrizations $x_i=\varphi_i(t_1,\dots,t_\nu)$ are exactly the solutions of a system of homogeneous equations. |
| 1099 | 600_2 | 83 | [78] | claim | unclear | eine {+stetige,+} monotone umkehrbar eindeutige Function welche für alle rationalen Argumente rational wird und auch alle rationalen Werte für rationale Argumente an- nimmt. | a continuous, monotone, one-to-one invertible function which becomes rational for all rational arguments and also takes all rational values for rational arguments. | Hilbert constructs a function by reading the binary expansion of x as a ternary expansion after a digit substitution, and argues that it is a continuous, monotone, bijective function mapping the rationals onto the rationals, but not analytic. |
| 1100 | 600_2 | 84 | 79 | claim | unclear | Auf der nebenstehenden Be- randung in der $z$ Ebene bleibt $u$ überall endlich und stetig | On the adjacent boundary in the z-plane, u remains everywhere finite and continuous. | A planned proof that a homogeneous polynomial admitting a multiplication theorem via bilinear forms must be a determinant after linear substitution, followed by a study of the potential given by the real part of the square root of log z and its boundedness on a domain in the z-plane. |
| 1101 | 600_2 | 85 | [80] | claim | unclear | Also überhaupt für alle Funktionen $f(x,y)$, die auf dem nebenstehende [-Ha-]Halbkreise die vorgeschriebenen Werte von $u$ haben, ist das Dirichlet- Integral $=\infty$. | So in general, for all functions $f(x,y)$ that have the prescribed values of $u$ on the adjacent semicircle, the Dirichlet integral is $=\infty$. | The page argues that the Dirichlet integral is infinite for every function taking prescribed boundary values on a half-disc, illustrated by conformal maps from the z-plane via the logarithm and a square root. |
| 1102 | 600_2 | 86 | 81 | claim | unclear | $(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{P}) = (\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{S}) = (\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{P}) = (\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{S})$ | In the plane, one has all intersection-point theorems if one assumes any of these equivalent axiom combinations: (A,D,P) = (A,D,S) = (A,C,P) = (A,C,S). | Hilbert lists groups of geometric axioms (incidence, congruence/motion, continuity/Archimedean, Desargues, special parallel axiom), states equivalences among combinations of them in the plane and in space, and then poses the problem of constructing π (and the cube root of 2) as accurately as possible with as few construction operations as possible. |
| 1103 | 600_2 | 86 | 81 | claim | unclear | $(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{P}) = (\mathfrak{A}\,\mathfrak{C}) = (\mathfrak{A}\,\mathfrak{S})$ | In space: (A,P) = (A,C) = (A,S). | Hilbert lists groups of geometric axioms (incidence, congruence/motion, continuity/Archimedean, Desargues, special parallel axiom), states equivalences among combinations of them in the plane and in space, and then poses the problem of constructing π (and the cube root of 2) as accurately as possible with as few construction operations as possible. |
| 1104 | 600_2 | 88 | 83 | claim | unclear | Der Nachweis der Existenz des Klassenkörpers und auch der Reciprocitätsgesetze ist in dem beson- deren Falle leichter, wenn man annimmt, dass der Klassenkörper $\underline{\text{Galoisch}}$ ist. | The proof of the existence of the class field and also of the reciprocity laws is easier in the special case when one assumes that the class field is Galois. | A pasted bibliographic slip citing Paolo Bonaventura's 1892 paper on the reciprocity theorem for biquadratic residues in the field (1, i√2), followed by notes on the existence of class fields and reciprocity laws and on proving the general biquadratic reciprocity law. |
| 1105 | 600_2 | 91 | [86] | claim | unclear | In der Ebene leicht zu beweisen: | In the plane easy to prove: | The page ends a note on a geometric distance problem, records claims that x^2+2=y^n and x^2+5=y^n have no integer solutions, and states Minkowski's theorem on convex surfaces with prescribed Gaussian curvature as a function of the normal, with a sketch of the plane case. |
| 1106 | 600_2 | 91 | [86] | claim | unclear | dann giebt es stets [-bis auf Translationen-] [-at-] eine und bis auf Translationen nur eine convexe Fläche deren Krümmungsmaass an der Stelle mit der Normale {+Gaussches+} $\alpha,\beta,\gamma$ gleich $f(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$ ausfällt | then there always exists one and, up to translations, only one convex surface whose Gaussian curvature at the point with normal α,β,γ equals f(α,β,γ) | The page ends a note on a geometric distance problem, records claims that x^2+2=y^n and x^2+5=y^n have no integer solutions, and states Minkowski's theorem on convex surfaces with prescribed Gaussian curvature as a function of the normal, with a sketch of the plane case. |
| 1107 | 600_2 | 92 | 87 | claim | unclear | Es giebt eine Reihe von Funktionen $\varphi_1, \varphi_2, ..$ von $x$; die ganz und rational in $x$ sind und so dass jede [-?-]stetige Funktion von $x$: $f(x)$ sich in eine Reihe von der Gestalt $f(x) = c_1\varphi_1 + c_2\varphi_2 + c_3\varphi_3 + \dots$ | There is a sequence of functions φ1, φ2, ... of x which are rational integral (polynomial) in x and such that every continuous function f(x) of x [can be expanded] into a series of the form f(x) = c1φ1 + c2φ2 + c3φ3 + ... | Short notes: a remark on curve closure, whether polyhedra can be bent, a Hurwitz extremal problem on polygons, a plan to treat the rotation of a fluid-filled body with Klein's parameters, a question on force fields with only closed orbits (Darboux–Halphen), and the start of a statement on expanding continuous functions in series of rational integral functions. |
| 1108 | 600_2 | 92 | 87 | claim | unclear | Falls $\mu \leqq 1$ sind es die regulären Polygone. So the pentagon is the first interesting case! | Short notes: a remark on curve closure, whether polyhedra can be bent, a Hurwitz extremal problem on polygons, a plan to treat the rotation of a fluid-filled body with Klein's parameters, a question on force fields with only closed orbits (Darboux–Halphen), and the start of a statement on expanding continuous functions in series of rational integral functions. |
| 1109 | 600_2 | 95 | [90] | claim | unclear | also man erhält nur ganze algebraische Zahlen. | so one obtains only algebraic integers. | Hilbert considers geometric constructions where only laying off segments straight or at a right angle counts as an operation (giving a ± b and √(a²+b²)), notes that only algebraic integers result, and asks how best to approximate given numbers such as π, angle trisection or 1/2 with a fixed number of operations, citing π ≈ √10 (Brahmagupta). |
| 1110 | 600_2 | 95 | [90] | claim | unclear | z.B. $\pi$ kann man durch 4 Operationen [Figur: rechtwinkliges Dreieck mit Hypotenuse $\sqrt{10}$] $\pi = \sqrt{10}$ $= 3,16$ sehr gut annähern. | E.g. π can be approximated very well by 4 operations as π = √10 = 3.16. | Hilbert considers geometric constructions where only laying off segments straight or at a right angle counts as an operation (giving a ± b and √(a²+b²)), notes that only algebraic integers result, and asks how best to approximate given numbers such as π, angle trisection or 1/2 with a fixed number of operations, citing π ≈ √10 (Brahmagupta). |
| 1111 | 600_2 | 96 | 91 | claim | unclear | Dass ebenso viel Punkte auf der Linie wie im Quadrat giebt ist sehr leicht. | That there are just as many points on the line as in the square is very easy. | Reflecting on Cantor's letters, Hilbert considers the problem of which collections may be regarded as 'finished' (complete) in set theory, and proposes formulating set-theoretic propositions without the word 'set' and treating terms such as 'irrational number' merely as names for things with specified properties. |
| 1112 | 600_2 | 97 | [92] | claim | unclear | Ein wissenschaftlicher Fortschritt ist häufig blos[?] die Ueberwindung eines Vorurteils. | A scientific advance is often merely the overcoming of a prejudice. | A short reflection that scientific progress often consists merely in overcoming a prejudice that had directed attention toward habitual ways of posing questions. |
| 1113 | 600_2 | 97 | [92] | claim | unclear | Man ist eben bis dahin an den nächstliegenden und fruchtbarsten Wahrheiten vorbeigegangen, weil das Vorurteil die Aufmerksamkeit auf die gewohnten Frage- stellungen richtete. | Until then, one had simply passed by the most obvious and most fruitful truths, because the prejudice directed attention toward the customary ways of posing questions. | A short reflection that scientific progress often consists merely in overcoming a prejudice that had directed attention toward habitual ways of posing questions. |

| 1126 | 600_2 | 109 | [104] | claim | unclear | Die Kritik wäre an sich unfruchtbar, daher muss sie so gehandhabt werden, dass sie das grosse aus der formalen Periode überkommene Material nicht blos durch Ausschalten reinigt, sondern durch Zu- sammenhalten verschiedener Gesichtspunkte, durch Einschlagen mannigfaltiger Wege, durch Zusammen- passen scheinbar verschiedenartiger Gegenstände, ver- bindet. Darin liegt die wahre Fruchtbarkeit und die  | Criticism would in itself be unfruitful. It must therefore be handled so that it does not merely purify the great material inherited from the formal period by eliminating things. It must also connect that material by holding different viewpoints together, by taking manifold paths, and by fitting together seemingly heterogeneous objects. Therein lies the true fruitfulness and the future of the crit | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: the mathematician experiments without the senses while the physicist works with sense impressions, the roots of mathematical strength do not lie in applications, and the 'critical period' of mathematics must unify viewpoints, which Klein seeks in geometry and Hilbert in analysis. |
| 1127 | 600_2 | 109 | [104] | claim | unclear | Die Wurzeln der Kraft in der Mathematik liegen nicht in den Anwendungen auf Physik, Astronomie etc. | The roots of the strength in mathematics do not lie in the applications to physics, astronomy, etc. | Philosophical remarks on mathematics: the mathematician experiments without the senses while the physicist works with sense impressions, the roots of mathematical strength do not lie in applications, and the 'critical period' of mathematics must unify viewpoints, which Klein seeks in geometry and Hilbert in analysis. |

| 1151 | 600_3 | 14 | 11 | claim | unclear | die Lösung ist die, dass die Menge aller Mengen nicht eine durch eine endliche Anzahl von Axiomen nach unserer axiomatischen Methode definirbare "consistente" Menge ist und nur für solche Menge gelten die Cantorschen Sätze. | the solution is that the set of all sets is not a "consistent" set definable by a finite number of axioms according to our axiomatic method, and Cantor's theorems hold only for such sets. | Hilbert argues that the axiomatic method is indispensable in set theory, resolving the paradox of the set of all sets by denying that it is a consistent axiomatically definable set, and then considers how to order the voting when several motions are pending at the end of a debate, listing the 8 possible outcomes for 3 motions. |

| 1153 | 600_3 | 16 | 13 | claim | unclear | Daher kann gewiss jede Funktion durch solche Funktion von 2 Veränderlichen [-ange-][?] beliebig angenähert werden, was für Zwecke der Nomographie ja vollständig ausreicht. | Hence every function can certainly be approximated arbitrarily closely by such functions of 2 variables, which is entirely sufficient for the purposes of nomography. | Hilbert discusses which functions of three variables can be built by successive composition of functions of two arguments, and proposes the root t of t^6 + x t^3 + y t^2 + z t + 1 = 0 as probably the simplest example of a genuine function of three variables, noting that a proof would likely be very hard. |
| 1154 | 600_3 | 16 | 13 | claim | unclear | Ebenso [-alle-] {+die+} Wurzeln von allen Gleichungen der Grade $2-5$, weil diese auf Gl. mit nur 2 Parametern führen. | Likewise the roots of all equations of degrees 2-5, because these lead to equations with only 2 parameters. | Hilbert discusses which functions of three variables can be built by successive composition of functions of two arguments, and proposes the root t of t^6 + x t^3 + y t^2 + z t + 1 = 0 as probably the simplest example of a genuine function of three variables, noting that a proof would likely be very hard. |
| 1155 | 600_3 | 17 | [14] | claim | unclear | Die Schar ist durch die Potenzreihen $p_n, \dots$ bestimmt. | The family is determined by the power series p_n, ... | Hilbert defines families of power series in three variables of the form f(x,y,z) = w_0 + w_1 z + ... with arbitrary coefficient functions w_0,...,w_{n-1} of x,y and determined later coefficients, calls the totality a function family of rank n, and then considers arbitrary functions of x,y after successive steps, ending with a substitution F(p,q) = W(p - p(0,0), q - q(0,0)). |
| 1156 | 600_3 | 19 | [16] | claim | unclear | Nun bilde man, was nicht schwierig ist eine Potenzreihe der 3 Veränderlichen $x, y, z$, die keine der unendlichen vielen Relationen $R^{(2)} = 0$ $R^{(3)} = 0$, $R^{(4)} = 0$, ... erfüllt, so ist [-diese-] durch diese Potenzreihe dargestellte Function noth- wendig eine eigentliche Function der 3 Veränderlichen $x, y, z$ | Now construct, which is not difficult, a power series in the 3 variables x, y, z that satisfies none of the infinitely many relations R^(2) = 0, R^(3) = 0, R^(4) = 0, ...; then the function represented by this power series is necessarily a genuine function of the 3 variables x, y, z. | Hilbert argues by counting arbitrary constants in power series that functions of three variables built in successive steps from functions of two variables (F^(2), F^(3), ...) satisfy infinitely many coefficient relations, so a power series in x, y, z satisfying none of these relations represents a genuine function of three variables. |

| 1303 | 600_3 | 125 | [122] | claim | unclear | In Zukunft wird das Operiren mit Begriffen vorherr- schen, wie es Cauchy und Riemann in- augurirt haben | In the future, operating with concepts will predominate, as Cauchy and Riemann inaugurated it. | Methodological remarks on mathematics: the earlier dominance of calculation versus a future dominance of operating with concepts, the reduction of quantity to quality in the theory of real numbers, the value of axiomatization including for physics, and a reply to the demand that everything be computed explicitly. |

| 1305 | 600_3 | 126 | 123 | claim | unclear | Ich füge hinzu dass sie oft nicht die schwierigsten sind. | I add that they are often not the most difficult. | Hilbert remarks that someone who solved the Riemann prime number problem would be welcome even without proper schooling, argues that applications should never decide the choice of problems, and quotes d'Alembert on abstract concepts being the clearest, adding that they are often not the hardest. |
| 1306 | 600_3 | 128 | 125 | claim | unclear | In der Thatsache, dass man die himmlischen Entfernungen mit den irdi- schen vergleichen kann und andererseits in der Vorstellung der Chemiker, dass die endlich fortgesetzte Teilung der Körper auf die Moleküle u. d. die der Moleküle auf die Atome führt, erblicke ich {+erst+} die experimentellen Belege für das Archimedische Axiom. | In the fact that one can compare celestial distances with terrestrial ones, and on the other hand in the chemists' conception that finitely continued division of bodies leads to molecules and that of molecules to atoms, I see for the first time the experimental evidence for the Archimedean axiom. | Methodological remarks: schematic generalisation is harmful compared to specialising fruitful problems, celestial and molecular facts as experimental support for the Archimedean axiom, and the relation between theory and the world of phenomena. |
| 1307 | 600_3 | 129 | [126] | claim | unclear | so erkennt man, dass [-?-] das dasselbe ist, wie das Problem alle 3 gliedrigen Gruppen von continuirlichen Transformationen aufzustellen (ohne aber die Differenzirbarkeit der die Transformation vermittelnden Funktionen vorauszusetzen) | One recognizes that this is the same as the problem of determining all 3-parameter groups of continuous transformations (without, however, assuming differentiability of the functions effecting the transformation). | Remarks on models as ends in themselves (citing Fritz Kötter's polemic against Klein–Sommerfeld on numerical computation), an analogy between the quadratic reciprocity law and hydraulic formulas, and a reflection that all thinking consists in finding simpler problems, illustrated by the parallel axiom and the determination of 3-parameter continuous transformation groups. |
| 1308 | 600_3 | 131 | [128] | claim | unclear | Diese wie jene müssen viel genauer richtig sein, weil kleine Abweichungen der Stetigkeitsaxiome wegen ihrer unendlich vielfachen An- wendung schon endliche Fehler geben. | These, like those, must be correct much more precisely, because small deviations in the continuity axioms already give finite errors on account of their infinitely repeated application. | Methodological reflections: the lasting value of old theories, an analogy between the continuity axioms of geometry and molecular hypotheses in physics (including the possible elimination of physical constants), and arguments for the axiomatic method. |
| 1309 | 600_3 | 131 | [128] | claim | unclear | die axiomatische Methode ist die definitive für den streng wissenschaftlichen Aufbau zu verwendende, weil einheitlich und weil Trennung der Theorie und Erfahrung durch sie allein streng und rein möglich ist. | The axiomatic method is the definitive one to be used for strictly scientific construction, because it is unified and because separation of theory and experience is possible strictly and purely through it alone. | Methodological reflections: the lasting value of old theories, an analogy between the continuity axioms of geometry and molecular hypotheses in physics (including the possible elimination of physical constants), and arguments for the axiomatic method. |
| 1310 | 600_3 | 132 | 129 | claim | unclear | Das wichtige ist, dass die Anzahl der Rechnungsoperationen unterhalb einer vor der Rechnung angebbaren endlichen Zahl liegen muss, wobei diese Zahl unabhängig von den besonderen Daten, den besondern Werten der Parameter etc sein muss. | The important thing is that the number of computational operations must lie below a finite number that can be specified before the computation, where this number must be independent of the particular data, the particular values of the parameters, etc. | Hilbert argues that a computational problem counts as solved only when the number of arithmetic operations is bounded in advance by a finite number independent of the particular data and parameter values, illustrating this with the sum of an arithmetic series and a geometric series. |
| 1311 | 600_3 | 132 | 129 | claim | unclear | Erst durch meine obige Forderung wird [-?-] jedes Problem erfassbar. | Only through my above requirement does every problem become graspable. | Hilbert argues that a computational problem counts as solved only when the number of arithmetic operations is bounded in advance by a finite number independent of the particular data and parameter values, illustrating this with the sum of an arithmetic series and a geometric series. |
| 1312 | 600_3 | 133 | [130] | claim | unclear | Bei allen nicht Archimedischen Schlüssen genügt stets zum Beweise eines bestimmten Satzes eine feste von der besonderen Figur oder dem besonderen Falle unabhängige Anzahl von Schlüssen. | For all non-Archimedean inferences, a fixed number of inferences, independent of the particular figure or the particular case, always suffices to prove a given theorem. | Hilbert contrasts proofs that use the Archimedean axiom, where the number of axiom applications can depend on the data, with non-Archimedean proofs that need a fixed number of steps, and then proposes finding a general method for stating problems precisely so that one can always decide whether they are solved. |

| 1322 | 600_3 | 137 | [134] | claim | unclear | Es giebt nicht nur eine geometrische Anschauung, sondern auch eine arithmetische, eine mechanische [?] und eine physiolo- gische Anschauung | There is not only a geometric intuition, but also an arithmetic, a mechanical [?] and a physiological intuition | A page of aphorisms on bad mathematical-physical theories, half-knowledge, spatial intuition, Cantor's set theory, mathematicians versus physicists, and the different kinds of intuition (Anschauung), with a reference to Helmholtz. |
| 1323 | 600_3 | 139 | [136] | claim | unclear | Wie unwesentlich das Wort dem Ton nach ist, sieht man: es kann durch beliebig andere con- ventionell (Geheimschrift – Telegraphen) oder historisch festgesetzte oder verabredete ersetzt werden; nur der Gedanke der selbe, die Invariante. | How inessential the word is as regards its sound can be seen from this: it can be replaced by arbitrary other words, whether conventionally fixed (cipher, telegraph) or historically established or agreed upon; only the thought stays the same, the invariant. | Loose reflections: a soldier's respect for a commander who shoots well, the inessential nature of words compared with the thought as 'invariant', whether practical activities such as gardening give better knowledge of geometric concepts, how interests in the same object differ, and whether geometric drawing trains geometric intuition. |
| 1324 | 600_3 | 143 | 1r | claim | unclear | wird von der 3 ten Ordng sein mit in Grade bestimmten Koefficienten etc. | [the differential equation] will be of 3rd order with coefficients of determined degree, etc. | Notes on proving irrationality of values of solutions of $y''=xy$, a proposed proof that signals propagate slower than light, a formula for the average price level in terms of money and circulation velocity, and a one-dimensional model of cohesion between dipole atoms. |

| 1339 | 600_2 | 94 | 89 | problem | solved on page | Wir lauten[?] die Lagrangeschen Differentialgleichungen z.B. zunächst für die Bewegung eines Punktes, wenn eine oder zwei Differentialgleichungen $f$ von $f$ der Gestalt $f(x', y, z, x', y', z', x'', y'', z'',$ $x''' x y''' z''' \ldots t) = 0$ gegeben sind. Also allgemeinster nicht- holonomer Fall! | We [seek?] the Lagrangian differential equations, e.g. first for the motion of a point, when one or two differential equations f of the form f(x', y, z, x', y', z', x'', y'', z'', x''', y''', z''', ... t) = 0 are given. So the most general non-holonomic case! | A note referencing Schubert's mathematical booklets (Göschen, Leipzig) on deriving Lagrange's differential equations for the motion of a point under general non-holonomic differential constraints, by introducing u, v, w as coordinates of a massless auxiliary point and applying Gauss's principle of least constraint. |
| 1340 | 600_3 | 36 | 33 | problem | solved on page | $f(x)$ ist stetig in $x$ und man weiss dass für jedes $a$: $f(x+a) - f(x)$ von $x$ unabhängig ist. Prove that f(x) is linear in x. | Notes on difference operators and iterated integrals, a proof that a continuous function whose difference f(x+a)-f(x) is independent of x is linear, a related argument using the integral F of f, and a problem of finding an invariant of a point set that singles out a particular point. |
| 1341 | 600_3 | 63 | [60] | problem | solved on page | Wenn man Integralgl. höheren Grades lösen will, so bietet sich als Beispiel $y''^2 = \lambda y y'$, wo das allgemeine Integral die Form $y = c\,F(x+c')$ haben muss. | If one wants to solve integral equations of higher degree, the example y''^2 = λ y y' presents itself, where the general integral must have the form y = c F(x + c'). | The page discusses minimum principles and stability criteria in statics and dynamics (F+V, F+T, d'Alembert and Hamilton principles), gives an example differential equation of higher degree with its general integral, and notes a plan to connect Cauchy's method for linear PDEs with constant coefficients to his integral equations and apply it to Maxwell's equations. |
| 1342 | 600_3 | 66 | 63 | problem | solved on page | Dann soll bewiesen werden, dass $A(wx)$ für alle $x$ richtig. | Then it is to be proved that A(wx) is true for all x. | A note on carrying over function theory systematically to potential theory in space (closed curves, logarithmic singularities, Green's function and boundary value problem for a circle), followed by a pasted slip on proving statements A(wx) for all x by induction-like axioms, the consistency of the system, and the existence of a successor for finite numbers. |
| 1343 | 600_3 | 67 | [64] | problem | solved on page | Nernst'sches Problem $k\frac{\partial^2 c}{\partial x^2} = \frac{\partial c}{\partial t}$ zu integriren, wenn für {+alle+} $t \geq 0$ und $x = 0$ $\;-k\frac{\partial c}{\partial x} = Ai$ (= const) ferner für alle $t \geq 0$ und $x = \infty$ $c = c_0$ (gg. Const) und für $t = 0$, $c = c_0$ für alle $x$ ist. | Nernst's problem: integrate $k\,c_{xx} = c_t$ when for all $t \geq 0$ and $x = 0$, $-k\,c_x = Ai$ (= const), further for all $t \geq 0$ and $x = \infty$, $c = c_0$ (a given constant), and for $t = 0$, $c = c_0$ for all $x$. | The page notes Nernst's diffusion problem with a closed-form solution, a question about two Newtonian mass points colliding along a line, questions about minimum principles in optics and Maxwell's electrodynamics, and the start of a remark on Poincaré's idea of periodic cycles on Riemann surfaces. |
| 1344 | 600_3 | 68 | 65 | problem | solved on page | Nernst Problem | Nernst problem (heat equation with $(c)_{t=0}=0$, $(\partial c/\partial x)_{x=0}=1$, $c=0$ at $x=\pi/2$). | The page ends a remark on finding covering surfaces and the uniformisation problem, solves Nernst's heat-conduction problem with a Fourier series, lists two proposed doctoral thesis topics, and begins describing the idea behind a continuity proof. |

| 1347 | 600_1 | 125 | [116] | question | solved on page | Wie erhalte ich nun $K(TST^{-1})$ aus $K(S)$ | How do I now obtain K(TST^{-1}) from K(S)? | Hilbert describes how to obtain the form K(TST^{-1}) from K(S) by a linear substitution of the variables and coefficients, relating ξ_1, ξ_2 to x_1, x_2 via coefficients β_{ik}. |
| 1348 | 600_2 | 70 | 65 | question | solved on page | wie kommt es, dass die Wissenschaft nicht an überfülltem Magen erstickt? | how is it that science does not suffocate from an overfull stomach? | Reflections on oral tradition alongside learning from books (Hilbert learned Weierstrass's theory from Hurwitz), on why science is not overwhelmed by publications, on teaching sharper tools early, and the start of a remark on mathematical disciplines arising from physics. |
| 1349 | 600_2 | 74 | 69 | question | solved on page | vielleicht gibt es ganze irreducible Gleichungen, die nach jeder Primzahl im Sinne der Congruenz reducible werden | perhaps there are integral irreducible equations that become reducible modulo every prime in the sense of congruence | The page discusses irreducible integer equations that become reducible modulo every prime, gives the example built from the square roots of 2 and 3, and proposes an alternative definition of an analytic function of two complex variables. |
| 1350 | 600_3 | 34 | 31 | question | solved on page | Welches [?] geschlossene Polyeder ent- spricht der Boyschen Fläche? | Which closed polyhedron corresponds to the Boy surface? | Hilbert sketches an argument that the points of greatest and least 'self-distance', defined by growing geodesic circles until they touch themselves, give closed geodesics on a surface; he then discusses polyhedral models of the Steiner surface and the Boy surface obtained from a modified octahedron. |
| 1351 | 600_3 | 42 | 39 | question | solved on page | Wenn $x = x(t)$, $y = y(t)$ eine Jordansche Curve, die ge- schlossen ist, darstellt und etwa der Nullpunkt ein innerer Punkt ist, wie kann man feststellen, ob der von $t=0 \ldots$ bis $t = 2\pi$ durchlaufene Sinn positiv ist? | If $x = x(t)$, $y = y(t)$ represents a closed Jordan curve, and say the origin is an interior point, how can one determine whether the sense traversed from $t=0$ to $t=2\pi$ is positive? | A remark on distinguishing layers of axioms in every science (with Hilbert's own foundations of geometry and the physicist as examples), followed by a method for deciding whether a closed Jordan curve around the origin is traversed in the positive sense. |

| 1353 | 600_3 | 91 | [88] | question | solved on page | Gefragt wird nach dem geometrischen Ort des Punktes Q. Answer: the perpendicular bisector of AB. | The page notes that certain decision questions about curves are answerable in principle yet as impractical as computing a remote digit of π^π, then gives a moral maxim, three mathematical trick problems with answers, and a plan for a theory of electrical conduction through gases. |
| 1354 | 600_3 | 100 | 97 | question | solved on page | Warum muss das stets wirklich passi- ren d.h. geschehen, was [-nicht-] logisch richtig aus richtigen Praemissen geschlossen ist | Why must whatever is concluded in a logically correct way from correct premises always actually happen, i.e. occur? | Hilbert argues that all sciences that produce lasting results ultimately supply material for mathematics, citing Einstein's 1912 letter to Sommerfeld, and proposes grounding logic in physical-mechanical considerations as an invariant kind of 'happening'; he then begins a thesis on applying the laws of probability to logic and mathematical statements. |
| 1355 | 600_3 | 104 | 101 | question | solved on page | Warum hat die Temperatur $\Theta$, aber nicht die Zeit $t$ einen absoluten Null- punkt? | Why does temperature Θ, but not time t, have an absolute zero point? | Philosophical notes on the application of Hilbert's theory against Kronecker's finitism, a maxim on avoiding overly elementary methods in proofs, how the logical connectives 'and', 'or', 'not' differ from geometric objects as things one can experiment with on paper, and why temperature but not time has an absolute zero. |
| 1356 | 600_3 | 106 | 103 | question | solved on page | Hat der Physiker Recht, wenn er sagt a. der Beweis für die Existenz der Lösung der Randwertaufgabe b. der Satz von der Möglichkeit der Entwickelung einer willkürlichen Funktion nach Eigenfunktionen interessire ihn nicht und habe für die Physik keine Bedeutung? | Is the physicist right when he says that (a) the proof of the existence of the solution of the boundary value problem and (b) the theorem on the possibility of expanding an arbitrary function in eigenfunctions do not interest him and have no significance for physics? | Hilbert reflects on modern mathematics, which seeks inner understanding through ideas and culminates in the axiomatic method, and then asks what physics needs from mathematics for its progress and for its logical foundation. |
| 1357 | 600_3 | 106 | 103 | question | solved on page | Was braucht der Physiker für den wirklichen Fortschritt der Physik? | What does the physicist need for the real progress of physics? | Hilbert reflects on modern mathematics, which seeks inner understanding through ideas and culminates in the axiomatic method, and then asks what physics needs from mathematics for its progress and for its logical foundation. |
| 1358 | 600_3 | 119 | [116] | question | solved on page | Wo fängt die Math. an? | Where does mathematics begin? | Hilbert reflects on the infinite as the real difficulty in mathematics, on where mathematics begins (concepts, axioms, logic), on restriction to essentials, on the uneven quality of journal articles, and on the value of reviewing (Referiren) in mathematics. |
| 1359 | 600_1 | 79 | [70] | plan | solved on page | Berechne so die $S$[?]-Funktion, [-ver-] möge $l\sin x$[?] $= \sum \mathfrak{B}_n x^n$ | Compute in this way the S-function, by means of l sin x = Σ B_n x^n. | The page ends a remark on infinitesimal transformations and invariants, citing Study's book on ternary forms. It then uses a contour integral to compute the power-series coefficients of a function as trigonometric integrals, and starts a study of first-order differential equations f(x, y, dy/dx) = 0 with polynomial f. |
| 1360 | 600_2 | 8 | 3 | plan | solved on page | Minkowskischen Satz von den ganzzahligen Substitutionen suche auf solche mit ganzen algebraischen Coefficienten zu erweitern. | Try to extend Minkowski's theorem on integral substitutions to substitutions with algebraic integer coefficients. | The page ends a remark on higher-than-quadratic forms, outlines extending Minkowski's theorem on integral substitutions to substitutions with algebraic integer coefficients using congruences modulo a prime ideal, and begins a note on Gauss's choice of arguments for mechanical quadrature. |
| 1361 | 600_2 | 15 | [10] | plan | solved on page | Nun soll berechnet werden, mit welcher Potenz von $y$ die Hessische von $f$ beginnt wenn man in derselben $x = \varphi(y)$ einsetzt. | Now it shall be computed with which power of y the Hessian of f begins when one substitutes x = φ(y) into it. | Computing the lowest power of y in the Hessian of a curve f after substituting x = φ(y), and concluding that exactly k-2 inflection points fall into the origin when the branch has expansion x = α_k y^k + ..., provided the origin is not singular. |
| 1362 | 600_2 | 25 | [20] | plan | solved on page | Es ist nur noch nöthig den dabei benutzten Hilfssatz zu beweisen. | It is only still necessary to prove the auxiliary lemma used there. | Hilbert completes a proof that, after multiplication by a suitable number c_1, every integer of a relative field can be written as a combination of powers with coefficients from the base field, proves the auxiliary lemma used, and starts relating the discriminant of the upper field to a determinant. |
| 1363 | 600_3 | 84 | 81 | plan | solved on page | Man kann nun dies durch directe Rechnung bestätigen und zugleich $\lambda$ finden, wie folgt: | One can now confirm this by direct computation and at the same time find λ, as follows: | Verifying by direct computation, using Legendre (spherical) function identities, that the exponent is μ=(n+1)^2 and determining the parameter λ via an auxiliary function Q(x) built from X_n and X_{n+1}. |

| 1368 | 600_1 | 33 | [24] | claim | solved on page | Der Beweis der obigen Sätze ist so: Man gehe in der gegeben [-Reihe-] convergenten Reihe soweit bis der Rest $< \frac{1}{2}$ und multiplicire alle Glieder selbst mit 2, dann gehe man bis der Rest $< \frac{1}{4}$ und multiplici- wieder mit 2 etc. | The proof of the above theorems is as follows: in the given convergent series, go far enough that the remainder is < 1/2, and multiply all the terms themselves by 2; then go far enough that the remainder is < 1/4, and multiply by 2 again, etc. | Notes on ratio-type convergence conditions for series (a_n/a_{n+1} > 1, n(a_n/a_{n+1} - 1) compared with 1), a remark that the boundary series diverges, a struck-out example, a reference to Weierstrass, and a sketch of a proof that multiplies the terms of a convergent series by 2 at successive stages. |

| 1376 | 600_1 | 74 | 65 | claim | solved on page | Wenn $f(x)$ eine ganze ganzzahlige Funktion $n^{\text{ten}}$ Grades ist, deren höchster Coefficient $=1$ ist, so kann man immer für $x$ eine ganze algebraische Zahl {+jedesmal[?]+} einsetzen, so dass $f(x)$ eine Einheit wird. | If f(x) is an integral polynomial with integer coefficients of degree n whose leading coefficient is 1, then one can always substitute for x an algebraic integer such that f(x) becomes a unit. | A short argument that a monic integer polynomial can always take a unit value at a suitable algebraic integer, followed by a polemic against the formalism of the Clebsch-Gordan school of invariant theory. |

| 1378 | 600_1 | 93 | [84] | claim | solved on page | Wenn die Resultante der $n$ Formen $f_1,\ldots f_n$ der $n$ Veränderlichen $x_1,\ldots x_n$ $\neq 0$ ist, so sind $x_1,\ldots x_n$ ganze algebr. | 1407 | 600_3 | 29 | [26] | claim | solved on page | Wenn für 2 {+stetige+} Functionen [-f(x)-] alle Integrale $\int_0^1 f(x)dx$, $\int_0^1\int_0^x f(x)\,dx\,dx$, $\int_0^1\int_0^x\int_0^x f(x)\,dx\,dx\,dx$, übereinstimmen, so stimmen die Functionen selbst überein. | If for 2 continuous functions all the integrals ∫_0^1 f dx, ∫_0^1∫_0^x f dx dx, ∫_0^1∫_0^x∫_0^x f dx dx dx, ... agree, then the functions themselves agree. | Remarks on comparing the simplicity of proofs by counting elementary steps, a programmatic note on turning differential geometry and physics into 'integral' versions, and a theorem that continuous functions agreeing in all iterated integrals over [0,1] are identical, proved via expansion in spherical (Legendre) functions. |

| 1412 | 600_3 | 73 | [70] | claim | solved on page | Einfachstes Beispiel $x'y' = s'^2$ [-...-] lösbar durch $x = e^t(w''-w')$, $y = e^{-t}(w''+w')$ $s = w + w''$. | Simplest example: x'y' = s'^2, solvable by x = e^t(w''-w'), y = e^{-t}(w''+w'), s = w + w''. | After a pasted note that the minimum criterion for the variational problem reduces to solving the boundary value problem for partial differential equations, Hilbert proposes that the calculus of variations be generalised into a theory of relations between functions, starting with 'diophantine' (ordinary) differential equations and the problem of uniformizing their solutions integrally. |
| 1413 | 600_3 | 78 | 75 | claim | solved on page | so ist auch $\beta \leqq \alpha$ und folglich $\alpha = \beta$ | so also $\beta \leq \alpha$ and consequently $\alpha = \beta$ | The page ends an argument that two spectral bounds alpha and beta must be equal, then replies to Frege's objection to Hilbert's axiomatic foundations: Hilbert says his axioms are statements about a basic relation of 'being assigned', so the primitive words can be exchanged. |
| 1414 | 600_3 | 81 | [78] | claim | solved on page | Bei den polaren Eigenfunktionen, die aus einer polaren Integralgleichung entspringen, können wir nicht schliessen, dass die Summe der Quadrate der Fourier[-x-]=Koeffizienten für eine beliebige stetige Funktion konvergirt, statt dessen aber dass stets $\frac{c_1^2}{\|\lambda_1\|} + \frac{c_2^2}{\|\lambda_2\|} + \cdots$ konvergirt | For the polar eigenfunctions arising from a polar integral equation, we cannot conclude that the sum of the squares of the Fourier coefficients converges for an arbitrary continuous function, but instead that c_1^2/\|λ_1\| + c_2^2/\|λ_2\| + ... always converges. | On polar integral equations: instead of convergence of the sum of squared Fourier coefficients, the series $\sum c_n^2/\|\lambda_n\|$ converges for a definite kernel, followed by the simplest example $u''+\lambda V(x)u=0$ with $V(x)$ the sign of $x$ on $[-1,1]$. |
| 1415 | 600_3 | 84 | 81 | claim | solved on page | und wegen der Entwickelung im Unendlichen folgt hier $\mu = +(n+1)^2$. | and because of the expansion at infinity it follows here that μ = +(n+1)^2. | Verifying by direct computation, using Legendre (spherical) function identities, that the exponent is μ=(n+1)^2 and determining the parameter λ via an auxiliary function Q(x) built from X_n and X_{n+1}. |
| 1416 | 600_3 | 85 | [82] | claim | solved on page | Also $Q$ sind die Nenner, wenn man $l\,\frac{1+x}{1-x}$ in der Weise durch Rationale Funktion $\frac{Z}{Q}$ annähert, dass $Q(+1)$ $: Q(-1)$ ein gegebenes Verhältniss haben. | Thus the Q are the denominators when one approximates log((1+x)/(1-x)) by rational functions Z/Q in such a way that Q(+1):Q(-1) have a given ratio. | Defining the denominator Q of a continued fraction (by analogy with spherical/Legendre functions) through the condition that the logarithmic terms in an integral cancel, followed by a remark that not every analytic function can be expanded in the polynomials P_n, shown via a Fourier-type expansion of f(x)/(x-α) whose convergence condition fails in general. |
